# Architecture review log

2026-09-19 — **#879 (`fix/879-withsameorigin-generic`, small lane):** `withSameOrigin` becomes return-type-preserving — a second generic `TRes extends Response` threads through `NextHandler`/`WrappedNextHandler`, so the wrapper returns `Promise<TRes | SameOriginForbidden>` instead of a flat `Promise<Response>` that widened all 58 wrapped routes (#865 D5 worked around it at the call site). The production diff is type-only; the function body is byte-identical and no call site changed. **The one non-obvious consequence, and the reason this is here:** `ci:admin-contract`'s walker (`scripts/ci/admin-contract-types.ts`) decided whether to descend into a wrapped handler by matching `responseBearing` on symbol NAME, so the newly generic `handler(req, …)` call — typed `Promise<TRes> | TRes` — read as not-a-Response, the walker stopped at the wrapper, and `/api/admin/impersonation/{start,end}` regenerated with `POST: 403` and a one-member response union instead of their real six. `responseBearing` now resolves a type parameter to its `getBaseConstraintOfType`, and a fixture case in `scripts/ci/__tests__/admin-contract-audit.test.ts` pins the descent. The generated artifacts under `docs/admin-api/` are byte-identical to before, which is the evidence the contract did not move. **Two named compromises, both from the spec review:** (a) THREE wrapped handlers annotate themselves `: Promise<Response>`, which pins `TRes = Response` and opts them out of the fix — all three return `NextResponse` on every path, so the annotation is stale, not deliberate; filed as #1327 and named in the wrapper's doc comment. **This entry first said ten**, from a grep of `: Promise<Response>` under `src/app/api` that counted seven route exports `withSameOrigin` never wraps — they are GETs outside the CSRF threat model or handlers calling `assertSameOrigin` inline. Count the WRAPPED sites, not the annotated ones; (b) `responseBearing` closes the type-PARAMETER case only — an intersection or an unresolved conditional is still invisible to it, which is pre-existing and unchanged by this work. One more fact worth not re-deriving: the two return-type guards are invisible to `pnpm test` — both rows go green under vitest against a fully reverted wrapper, so `pnpm typecheck` is their only gate.

- **PR #1306** — client-role grant sweep, migration 122 (2026-09-18, squash `fa52991cb8fb467337ab7d4300e642b1f0b6a5a9`; issue #1176, ticket 1 of 3). `anon`/`authenticated` lose TRUNCATE, REFERENCES, TRIGGER and MAINTAIN on every `public` table and in the table-owning role's schema-level default table privileges, and lose every privilege on the twenty deny-all tables (RLS on, zero policies). A `DO` guard refuses unless `current_user` owns every table; MAINTAIN is issued only on PostgreSQL ≥ 17. No RLS flag, policy, column grant, `service_role` or owner privilege moves; no code outside the guard suite. **Applied staging 2026-09-17T13:06:16Z, production 14:07:59Z**, both verified by introspection twice and proven in lockstep by byte-identical pre- and post-apply ACL snapshots. Review: 3 Codex plan rounds APPROVED, a full branch wave plus verification round APPROVED, red team CLEAR at round 3 (owner-authorized) with the PG17 branch mutation-tested on a disposable `postgres:17` container. Three P2s ship open — no PG17 CI lane (RT-4), one date-scoped clause since corrected (RT-5), and a round-2 rendering gap (RT-6) — carried by #1307. Two tooling defects surfaced and were fixed en route: #1302/#1303 (the red-team Operator-continuation predicate claimed every branch cut from `main`) and the `GIT_*` leak into the runner suite's own process. Counter 3→4. Next pass: whether the deny-all posture deserves a registry-driven guard (#1300) rather than per-migration REVOKEs.

2026-09-08 — **feat/1184-admin-get-fold (pre-merge):** The nine role-only admin
GET handlers fold onto `requireAdminWithAal2`, and `ci:admin-aal2`'s ROUTES
contract widens from the four mutation verbs to every exported handler under
`src/app/api/admin/**` (#1184, ADR 0025 D5). This is the compensating control
D6 spends: once #1185 lets a bearer through `src/proxy.ts` branch 4, the route
gate is the ONLY admin check on that request, and what makes that acceptable is
CI proving every handler has one. Nine route files, six page files, one audit,
one new test-only helper; no migration, no flag, no dependency, no new writer.
Four points are the reviewable ones. (1) **Widening the bound HANDLERS is the
opposite act from widening the credited SHAPES.** The first tightens a
fail-closed rule, the second would loosen it, and only the first happened — the
three gate shapes and their resolves-to-`CANONICAL` provenance check are
byte-identical. `HANDLER_VERBS` binds `HEAD` and `OPTIONS` although no admin
route exports either (preflight is answered in `src/proxy.ts` since #1186), so a
future admin `OPTIONS` export becomes a review moment instead of an invisible
front door; both are pinned by fixtures, because a verb the tree does not use is
exactly the verb a wrong list omits silently. (2) **The audit will no longer
report clean about a tree it could not read.** `collectNamedFiles` swallows a
`readdir` failure per directory and keeps walking its siblings, so one
unreadable subtree hid its routes while the other 34 scanned green — a zero-file
floor does not close that. The collectors now take an OPTIONAL error sink (their
sink-less behaviour, and its unit-test pin, unchanged) and `main()` fails on any
recorded traversal error, zero route files, or zero pages. Found by the
plan-skeptic round, not by the gate. (3) **Page rule P2 flipped six pages and
falsified the audit's own worked example.** `/admin/overview`,
`/admin/businesses`, `/admin/businesses/[id]`, `/admin/costs`, `/admin/jobs` and
`/admin/notifications` now pass `{ aal2: true }`, because a server page reads
`sql` directly and a weaker page gate is a parallel read path around the route's
step-up. `/admin/businesses/[id]` was the header's example of "P2 is NOT 'a page
hosting mutations must be AAL2'" and its recorded reason — plain parity with a
plain GET — is no longer true, so the paragraph now teaches the rule with
`/admin/businesses/new` (a form page with NO sibling route that opts into `aal2`
anyway) and keeps the old case as a dated historical note. A security audit that
teaches its rule with a false citation is worse than one that teaches it with
none. (4) **The #1183 mock-shim debt is paid.** Twenty-two admin suites carried
a byte-identical copy of the gate-dependency shim, verified by hash, and this
ticket would have added seven more; it now has one owner at
`src/lib/auth/__tests__/helpers/admin-gate-mocks.ts`, whose miss mode #1183
named — a silently vacuous authorization test — is guarded two ways: the helper
derives **Admin transport** from the request through the PRODUCTION
`isBearerRequest` rather than a knob (so a "bearer ok" case cannot reproduce the
cookie case's 200 without sending the header), and `admin-gate-mocks.test.ts`
pins that none of the nine folded suites stubs `@/lib/auth/admin-mfa-gate`.
Declared behaviour changes, both accepted directions: an `aal1` admin is refused
`mfa_required` on all nine reads and on the six pages, and a non-auth failure is
the gate's clean `internal_error` 500 instead of `requireRoleOr401`'s re-throw —
`costs`'s hand-written catch, which answered 401 for ANY non-`AuthError`, is
deleted with it. Nothing else is unified onto the gate.

**The first of those is only safe because `MFA_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` is on in
BOTH environments, and that fact must be read from the environment rather than
from this repo.** #1182's resolution comment (2026-09-08T23:35Z) records the
owner running the production half directly, from a production-scoped shell,
AFTER PR #1216 merged — so `env-vars.md:653`, the #1182 entry below, and commit
`cca34475` all predate it and all still say staging only. Verified live for this
branch instead: `vercel env ls production` shows `MFA_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` on the
Production environment created that day (value stored Encrypted, so the listing
proves presence and creation time; the value is attested by the owner's
password + TOTP sign-in rendering `/admin/overview` on production). A red-team
pass raised the stale documents as a P0 against this entry, which is the correct
instinct and the reason the sentence now names its evidence. Correcting
`env-vars.md` is its own ticket. The merge counter is unchanged until this branch
is merged.

2026-09-08 — **feat/1186-admin-api-cors (pre-merge):** CORS for the **Admin API**
(#1186, ADR 0026), the last edge-level blocker on the admin split. Nothing in this
repo admitted a cross-origin caller before this, so the **Admin app** could not
reach a single admin route and ADR 0025's dual-mode gate was inert. Four
production files, no migration, no flag, no dependency, one new server-only env
var (`ADMIN_APP_ORIGINS`, registered in both inventories). Four design points are
the reviewable ones. (1) **The seam is decision-out, headers-in.**
`src/lib/config/admin-origins.ts` owns which origins are admitted;
`src/proxy.ts` stays the only writer of an `Access-Control-*` header (ADR 0026
D1), so the glossary sentence and invariant #12 are both satisfied without the
proxy growing a parser. The block runs FIRST inside `proxy()`, before
`isProtectedRoute` — a preflight that reached branch 4 would 401 from a cookie the
browser may not send, and the browser would report an unreadable CORS failure
rather than a status. (2) **The existing body moved verbatim into `handleProxy`**
so the exported `proxy()` can stamp `Vary: Origin` and the echoed
`Access-Control-Allow-Origin` on EVERY `/api/admin/**` response. Four exits carry
one today and #1185/#1190 add more; hand-stamping call sites is a defect waiting
for the fifth. Branch 4's cookie/bearer logic is untouched — that is #1185. (3)
**`isSelfOrigin` becomes the single owner of the own-origin comparison**, shared by
CSRF and the CORS decision (ADR 0026 D4), and the allowlist deliberately does NOT
flow the other way: `assertSameOrigin` still refuses an allowlisted Admin app
origin and a loopback origin, pinned by a test, because the alternative silently
exempts every allowlisted origin from CSRF. The implicit self-allowance is
load-bearing in the other direction — without it every **Legacy admin portal**
mutation 403s, since a same-origin `POST` carries an `Origin` header. (4) **Two
rules are code, not configuration**: an entry containing `*` throws at read (which
is what keeps ADR 0026 D7's CSRF argument true), and loopback is decided
TERMINALLY by `!isProductionEnvironment()` so a listed `http://localhost` cannot
re-admit it in production. The lane predicate is `isProductionEnvironment()` —
a non-empty `VERCEL_TARGET_ENV` trusted verbatim, falling back to
`NODE_ENV === 'production'` only when that signal is missing — never
`requiresProductionConfig()`, which would have made the rule inert on staging,
the one environment ADR 0024 D7 built it for. Governance surface: one new file
under `src/lib/config/` with its `docs/directory` row. Three Codex plan-skeptic
rounds to APPROVED, 21 of 22 round-1 findings accepted (the owed test list grew
19 → 40 as a result). Test surface: 23 new CORS cases in `proxy.test.ts`, 19 new
cases across `admin-origins.test.ts` / `flags.test.ts` / `csrf.test.ts`, and 4
new `isAdminApiRoute` cases in `protected-routes.test.ts` (counts re-derived from
execution three times — an `it(`-grep misses `it.each`, which is what made the
first two attempts wrong). Red team: **70 mutations over THREE rounds**, all 13
named security properties killed; two P0s in the TEST surface (an unreachable
scheme-guard fixture, an Edge-safety pattern that could never match) fixed in
round 1's triage, and round 2 then caught the round-1 fix for the adversarial
lane finding being itself wrong. Adversarial review added two: `isProductionEnvironment` now fails CLOSED
on a missing `VERCEL_TARGET_ENV`, because a Vercel project with system variables
switched off would otherwise admit loopback in production; and the parser's throw
is CAUGHT at the proxy boundary and mapped to the ordinary 403 refusal, so a
misconfigured list is an admin-wide denial rather than an unhandled proxy
exception (a platform 500 with no CORS envelope) for the life of the deployment.
Counter: not incremented — this is a pre-merge trace, and the number is the architecture
pass's judgement.

2026-09-08 — **feat/1178-mint-admin (pre-merge):** The first admin-minting path
(`scripts/mint-admin.ts`, #1178). Every admin account to date was made by hand
in two halves that a hand process can half-succeed at, and the database has no
guardrail: `users_email_key` and `users_auth_id_key` are unique per VALUE, so
two admin identities for one human are legal. The script creates the Supabase
Auth login, then the `users` row (`role='admin'`, `account_id` NULL) and its
`admin_audit_log` row in ONE transaction, or leaves neither. No migration, no
flag, no new env var, no route — the service-role key makes it a script by
construction. Three design points are the reviewable ones. (1)
`admin_audit_log.actor_user_id` is NOT NULL and #1182 revokes every admin before
re-minting with this script, so there may be no other admin to name: the minted
admin is its OWN actor, resolved inside the same transaction so the FK holds
without touching migration 117 — the shape `mfa_factor_enrolled` already uses,
with the operator in the payload. (2) Compensation is conditional. A rejected
`sql.begin` does not prove a rollback, and neither does an absent row on a
second connection, so one reconciliation read precedes every compensating
`deleteUser` and the Auth identity is deleted only on a CONFIRMED rollback (a
refusal the script raised, or a class 22/23/42 SQLSTATE); an ambiguous outcome
preserves it, because deleting the identity behind a committed `users` row
leaves an admin who can never sign in. (3) The target guard requires
`--confirm-ref` to EQUAL the ref parsed out of BOTH the Supabase host and the
database connection, so a mismatched credential pair cannot be confirmed, and
`staging-guard.ts`'s production denylist is reused unweakened and runs first.
Governance surface touched: two new suites under `scripts/ci/__tests__/`, which
is where every `scripts/` module is tested and the only `scripts/` path
`vitest.config.ts` includes. Three Codex plan-skeptic rounds, seven findings
accepted; 31 tests (28 unit + 3 live-DB). Counter: not incremented — this is a
pre-merge trace, and the number is the architecture pass's judgement.

2026-09-06 — **fix/415-failover-claim-before-send (pre-merge):** Move the
`NotificationFailoverPoller` admin-alert claim to claim-before-send — the claim
row commits under the advisory lock, Resend is called OUTSIDE the transaction
with a per-claim idempotency key (`admin-alert:<claimId>`), and a failed send
KEEPS the claim (the Resend SDK folds transport failures into
`application_error`, so no thrown error proves non-acceptance). Behavior

next 2 h cycle (admin-only alerts). The other three #415 sites
(`edit-publish.ts`, `UpdateStripeOverageWorker.ts`,

2026-09-07 — **fix-1171-job-outbox-rls (MERGED, PR #1175; counter +1 → 20):** Migration 119 lands deny-all RLS on `job_outbox`, closing the last staging/prod RLS drift. Introspection of both projects found `job_outbox` was the ONLY table of the 54 in schema `public` whose `relrowsecurity` differed — prod true, staging FALSE, zero policies on both — while `anon` and `authenticated` held Supabase's default table privileges on both. RLS off plus `anon` INSERT is not disclosure alone: `OutboxPoller` dispatches whatever rows it finds without asking who wrote them, so anyone holding the public staging anon key could enqueue a job. **This is migration 107's deferred half** — prod (2026-04-05) shipped with the Supabase `ensure_rls` DDL event trigger, staging (2026-05-30) was provisioned after Supabase dropped it, and 107 removed the GENERATOR while deliberately leaving its past output for a reviewed migration of its own. Convergence is upward. Safe for the worker — confirmed against the live catalogues, not argued: every application connection authenticates as `postgres`, which both owns the table and holds BYPASSRLS; no supabase-js, no `.rpc(`, no view, function, trigger, publication or Edge Function over the table; zero column ACLs; FORCE stays off, asserted in catalogue and file text, because setting it would deny the outbox to its only writer. **Registries unchanged, by owner decision (2026-09-07):** `job_outbox` stays `REVIEWED_NOT_TENANT_SCOPED` and out of `TENANT_TABLES`, because membership also switches on `ci:tenant-scope`, which demands an `account_id`/`business_id` predicate on every binding — the table carries neither column and 55 files under `src/` + `worker/` (excluding `__tests__/`) bind it in SQL, so membership would cost dozens of allowlist entries and audit nothing real (the same measure-first refutation the 2026-08-18 pass recorded for the four `TENANT_TABLES` candidates). `ci:rls-audit` therefore does not cover this table and the boundary's guard is `src/lib/shared/__tests__/migration-119-job-outbox-rls.integration.test.ts`, the role 117's suite plays for `admin_audit_log`. **The review wave is the story of this branch.** Five axes returned 23 findings; 22 accepted, 1 rejected with reasoning. Four axes independently killed the same false claim — that production "has run this exact posture for five months" — when prod has run the RLS half only and holds the client grants still, so the empirical argument never covered the REVOKE, the one statement that could break the worker. Red team's P0 was proven by mutation rather than argued: `REVOKE ALL ON job_outbox FROM <owner>` left `relacl = {}` and the privilege case still PASSED, because the local and CI owner is the container superuser and `has_table_privilege` is unconditionally true for one — and after an earlier deviation dropped the `service_role` arms, that case was the suite's only detector of a widened REVOKE. It now reads `relacl`/`attacl` through `aclexplode`, which is superuser-proof and covers every privilege type: measured on the container, `GRANT TRUNCATE … TO authenticated` returns `f, f` under the old form and `authenticated | TRUNCATE | table` under the new. TRUNCATE is the case that matters, being exempt from row policies entirely. A P1 showed the file-text guard counted line starts rather than statements, so `SELECT 1; REVOKE … FROM service_role;` on one line evaded it; it now parses top-level statements and pins the revoke by EXACT match, and all six widening mutations are killed. Two more: the ALTER is not a lock-free no-op (`ATExecSetRowSecurity` has no early-out, `AT_EnableRowSecurity` takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE) so it is now guarded on `relrowsecurity`, which makes production a true no-op instead of an aspirational one; and the rollback plan would have DISABLED RLS on production, manufacturing the staging vulnerability there mid-incident — it is now per-environment, since the two projects do not share a pre-119 baseline. One migration, one new test file, no production TypeScript, no flag, no dependency. **Applied to staging 2026-09-07T11:42Z and production 2026-09-07T12:0xZ, both verified by introspection; lockstep proven by hash — one identical query over all 54 `public` tables returns `3404cf07434a4d4ad9670f62ce573556` on BOTH projects.** Merged as PR #1175. Two surface amendments used of two, both recorded with reasons. The `/feature` `reviewed` transition was NOT set and was not forced: `red-team-status.mjs` correctly refuses it because each pass's fixes moved the tree past the sidecar it was written against and the intake was amended twice — the five passes' reports under `docs/reviews/red-team/1171-*/` are the evidence instead. Residual scope carried to issue #1176: 21 production and 20 staging tables still hold client-role grants behind deny-all RLS, and `anon` holds TRUNCATE on 49 of 50 tables.

2026-09-07 — **docs/1163-1165-admin-split-decisions (MERGED, PR #1194; counter unchanged):** The last three decision tickets on wayfinder map #1159 resolved as ADRs 0027–0029. **All three tickets carried a false premise, and finding them was the work.** #1163 listed six admin pages as having an inline `sql` read; four had none — the `sql` import is only `requireAdminPage`'s first argument (invariant #15) — so the decision surface was two gaps, not five, and five unnecessary GET routes were avoided. #1164 asked to enumerate the Admin API's error codes; there is no vocabulary to enumerate, because 37 distinct `{ error: … }` strings live in admin routes and `errorResponse()` is used in ZERO of them, so the contract promises status codes plus prose instead and normalization becomes a separate ticket. #1165 listed six sites naming `/admin/overview`; there are eleven, and three of the five missed are on pages ADR 0024 D5 keeps forever (`admin/login`'s `defaultRoute`, `EndImpersonationButton`, `admin/error.tsx`) — a survivor pushing at a deleted page. Also settled: the impersonation preselect the ticket proposed cannot work, because `page-gate.ts:59` builds `?from=` from the page's STATIC route shape and drops a query parameter at the login door. Decisions of record: the batch summary FOLDS onto `GET /api/admin/businesses/[id]` rather than taking a third route (`DB_POOL_MAX=3`; the #773 fan-out re-appears at the HTTP layer where `ci:pool-fanout` is blind); the **Admin API contract** is GENERATED from existing code (`tsc --emitDeclarationOnly` over a DTO barrel + `z.toJSONSchema()` for the 14 routes that already have request zod — zod is `^4.4.3`, no new dependency) and gated by `ci:admin-contract` in the `ci:directory-map` posture; one server-only `ADMIN_APP_URL` behind one accessor, all eleven sites switching at overview parity. Glossary coins **Admin API contract** and sharpens **Legacy admin portal** with the exact end-state FILE set. Documentation only — no production TypeScript, migration, dependency, flag, or writer, so the counter is unchanged. Cut: #1189–#1193.

2026-09-07 — **docs/1168-admin-app-starter (counter unchanged):** Wayfinder decision ticket #1168 on map #1159 resolved as ADR 0030. **The ticket's recommended answer assumed a handshake that does not exist**: `GET /api/admin/overview` gates on `requireRoleOr401` (cookie-only, role-only, `route.ts:14`), `src/proxy.ts:139` 401s any cookie-less `/api/admin*` request before the route loads, no route reads `Authorization`, and no `Access-Control-*` header is written anywhere — so the starter's proof waits on #1184, #1185 and #1186 reaching staging. The starter therefore ships in TWO gates: Gate A (app, login, own MFA challenge, refresh middleware, shell, fetch seam, staging deploy) has no blocking edge and is where hand-over happens; Gate B (bearer read, CSRF POST, `aal1` refusal, refused origin) is blocked. **Token plumbing is HYBRID, and the fog patch is closed**: a server component's forwarded bearer carries no `Origin`, so ADR 0026 D4 leaves it untouched and `withSameOriginUnlessBearer` is never reached — reads pay no preflight and CORS stays on the mutation path only. Three more premise corrections: the repo is not empty (#1167 put 33 files there), so "starter vs empty repo" was never the choice; the exported kit hardcodes relative `/api/admin/...` in two files (`nav-counts-query.ts` polls one every 60 s) and would break the shell on first paint; and `ui-kit/lib/mfa-path.ts`'s exported comment proposes pointing the challenge at this app's origin, which cannot work because AAL is session-bound (#1160) and would leave the Admin app `aal1` forever. Also settled: three public variables and **no runtime secret**, which turns ADR 0024 D1 from a promise into an inspectable property; the Admin app reads its own `users` row under `users_self_select` (migration 006) for CHROME only, never as a boundary; and MFA enrollment is the developer's first page, not the starter's, because it needs no Admin API. Glossary coins **Admin app fetch seam** and sharpens **Admin app**. Documentation only — no production TypeScript, migration, dependency, flag, or writer, so the counter is unchanged. Cut: #1196, #1197.

2026-09-07 — **docs/1169-admin-retirement-order (counter unchanged):** The LAST decision ticket on wayfinder map #1159 resolved as ADR 0031, closing the map. **The ticket's retirement checklist was wrong in both directions.** Its five cited tests are three different kinds of thing, and only some break on deletion: `console-admin-palette`, `runbook-empty-polish` and `no-yellow-star-colors` `readFileSync` the file (hard pins, all three cited line numbers resolve); `server-client-value-imports` and `scanAdminPageFile` are DERIVED and self-adjust; and the `admin-aal2-audit.test.ts` "fixtures" plus `db-imports-audit.test.ts:103` pass a path STRING with synthetic page text (`ungatedPage`/`plainPage`/`aal2Page`) and never read a real file — so the checklist asked all sixteen retirement PRs to update two things that cannot fail. **Two hard pins were missing**: `src/app/admin/__tests__/page-chrome.test.ts` (a static page+client table read from disk) and `runbook-import-consent.test.ts` (the LARGER of the two runbook tests; the ticket cites only the smaller). Two more corrections: `ci:directory-map` does not scan `src/app/**` (`directory-map-audit.ts:51`), so the ticket's named enforcement for the `docs/directory/app-admin.md` row does not exist; and `src/components/admin/nav-config.ts`'s fifteen hrefs were absent from the checklist. **That last claim was overstated in the merged ADR and corrected the same day** (`docs/1169-nav-guard-correction`): `AdminSidebar.test.tsx` already carries four nav-versus-filesystem cases that walk `src/app/admin` for `page.tsx` files, and `points every nav href at a real page` (`:221-225`) fails on exactly this, so a stale entry turns CI red rather than shipping a 404. Looking for the guard also found a pin nobody had listed: `NON_NAV_PAGES` (`:189`) names `/admin/businesses/new`, and `keeps the excluded-page list honest` (`:227-231`) fails when that page is deleted, so tranche 3 drops that entry too. The lesson generalises past this branch — **the finding was 'absent from the checklist', and it was published as 'unguarded' without checking**; the two are different claims and only the first was measured. `/admin/businesses/[id]` is ELEVEN client components, not seven. Decisions of record: sixteen pages retire in **four tranches**, and the first (`/admin/security` + `/admin/runbook`) needs no Admin API at all, so deletions start before the bearer path opens; **Page parity** is coined with a machine half (route-set equality read from the generated **Admin API contract**) and a human half (screenshots at 390/1440 + sign-off), and a dropped route is a spec change said out loud; the silently-stale references are swept ONCE at the end rather than sixteen times, with a recorded owner reservation about `app-admin.md` rows aging in place; and the cookie-mode removal is cut NOW (#1203) blocked by the last tranche, which graduates the map's second fog patch. Four tickets cut, ten pages recorded rather than pre-cut — sixteen near-identical tickets before an owner priority exists is tracker noise. Closing this releases #1192 and #1193, which both carried `Blocked by: #1169`. Documentation only — no production TypeScript, migration, dependency, flag, or writer, so the counter is unchanged. Cut: #1200, #1201, #1202, #1203.

pass, the orchestrator reminds the user to schedule an architecture review
session (the user's call — never started automatically). A completed pass
resets the counter to 0 and adds a row below.

(entry at the bottom of this file). Full artifacts:
`docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-09-12/`.

> ## The counting rule is STILL under repair — do NOT hand-count this file
>
> The 2026-09-12 pass measured the prior window at **83** against a stated
> **21** — a 177-merge window, the largest yet, and the **seventh consecutive
> wrong counter**. H7 (filed 2026-08-26) did not land; the error grew from 4
> to **62** in seventeen days.
>
> All four reasons the 2026-08-26 pass measured still hold at HEAD:
>
> 1. **Two accepted locations** — the bullet list and the bottom table.
> 2. **No stable key.** Entries appear under PR number, issue number, branch
>    name, or bare prose.
> 3. **The audit's trigger is WIDER than the counting rule.** `ci:merge-ledger`
>    fires on production **OR GOVERNANCE** files; the rule counts production
>    **only**. **17 merges in the 2026-09-12 window** were each *required* by
>    CI to add a line and must *not* increment. They sit in the bottom table
>    beside counting merges, unmarked.
> 4. **"Lines added" is not a unit** — it ranged 1 to 36 per merge.
>
> **This file predicted its own failure twice inside that window**, which is
> the strongest evidence yet that the defect is structural rather than one of
> discipline: the 2026-09-01 note below ("*recovered MECHANICALLY rather than
> by reading this file*") and the 2026-09-03 bullet ("*The count lags reality
> — #1069, #1071, #1073, #1074, #1075, #1097, #1098 and #1099 all merged with
> production changes and no entry… reconciling them is the next architecture
> pass's job*"). **The reconciliation was 62, not 8.** The bullet list stopped
> being maintained entirely after 2026-09-04 — the last **39** counted merges
> (#1140 → #1270) have no bullet at all, including the whole Admin split wave
> and Owner Operator Run A.
>
> **One refinement the 2026-09-12 window adds:** only **3 of 83** counting
> merges had a comment-only production diff. **80 of 83 changed executable
> code.** The file rule over-counts by **3.6%** — cheap. Keep the file rule.
>
> **The fix is unchanged (H2, formerly H7):** one owner — the bottom table —
> keyed by **merge SHA**, with a `counts` boolean set by `isProductionFile`
> (`scripts/ci/merge-ledger-audit.ts:96`). `classifyDiff` (`:111-117`) already
> computes it on every run and discards it. Then the counter is a `grep -c`,
> and the governance-only rows classify themselves.
> `docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-09-12/04-handoff-prompts.md` H2.
>
> **The record, unchanged and worth keeping:** the 2026-08-24 pass reported
> this counter correct for the first time in five passes and credited
> `ci:merge-ledger`. That audit's own header
> (`merge-ledger-audit.ts:38-43`) explicitly declines to own the number.
> **The audit works exactly as designed; the counter was never in its scope.**
> Seven passes, six wrong counts, is the expected output of the current
> design — not a discipline failure.

Merges since the 2026-08-26 pass — **CLOSED by the 2026-09-12 pass.** Retained

- **PR #1276** — Owner Operator T8: the staging flip and its first live turn (2026-09-13, squash `099409ae2`; the line was written pre-merge by the branch). `OWNER_OPERATOR_ENABLED` is ON in staging (both lanes) and off in production; Sentry rule 11 exists in both projects. Production files: `prompt.ts`, `catalog.ts` (the `inputShape` field on every proposable), `constants.ts` (comment only). The first live Operator turn on staging failed `model_output_invalid` on BOTH pinned providers, because nothing declared the output shape to the model — the LLM seam sends no JSON schema to a provider and validates with zod only after the completion arrives, and prompt `2026-09-11.2` said "the declared schema" without declaring one. Fix: `prompt.ts` spells `OperatorModelOutputSchema` in the system prompt (version `2026-09-13.1`) and `catalog.ts` gives every proposable an `inputShape` projected as `allowedOperations[].input`, so the prompt carries no second list of ids; `prompt.test.ts` pins every key and segment type read FROM the schema. Plus red-team RT-B8's discriminating case and the rule-11 docs. Sentry rule 11 built in both projects; `OWNER_OPERATOR_ENABLED=true` on staging (both lanes), production off. **What the next pass should look at:** the seam declares NO structured-output contract to any provider — the triage, judge and solicitation tasks survive on trivial schemas; a per-task JSON-schema hand-off (OpenAI `response_format`, Anthropic tool input) would make the shape a provider guarantee instead of a prompt promise. No migration, no new dependency. The counter is the next pass's business.
- **PR #1273** — Owner Operator v1 Run B: Apply, Decline, receipts, the Operator screen (2026-09-13, squash `34857b73b`; the pre-merge ledger line below was written by the branch itself under surface amendment #2). No migration, no env var, no flag flip, no new dependency. **New single owners:** `src/app/api/operator/gate.ts` (the one operator mutation gate, order flag → same-origin → writable owner → impersonation → uuid; born from CI's fallow duplication gate reversing a standards REJECT) and `src/lib/admin/impersonation/cookie.ts` (`hasImpersonationCookie`, pass H1-B). **Allowlists moved:** `/api/operator` joined `isProtectedOwnerApiRoute` (H1-A); the PgBouncer JSONB parse-site allowlist 23 → 25. **Deleted concept:** the dashboard-DTO reuse for the summary panel (H1-D, refuted). **What the next pass should look at:** the five client hooks beside one page (`useOperatorConversation`, `useTurnPoll`, `useProposalWrites`, `useTurnSubmission`, `useOperatorUrl`) — a decomposition forced by fallow's hook-count complexity score rather than by a seam; the OutboxPoller's two Run A open items (spec §14 CA1 stranded turn, CA4 predecessor-wait clock) now that a screen polls them; `declineProposal` living in the row module (standards F7, rejected as "no new file"); red-team RT-B8 (the second-message refetch is unmeasured). The first executor on the Owner Operator. `src/lib/agents/operator/apply.ts` (lock-before-check Apply transaction: `businesses` row FOR UPDATE, then the proposal; three-way text **Version token** compare; the operation's own fields through the shared voice writer; the **Receipt** written in the same transaction; sibling pending Proposals marked stale) and `version-token.ts` (one owner for the token shape, its JSONB parse site and its equality); `declineProposal`; `POST /api/operator/proposals/:id/{apply,decline}` behind flag-first 404, same-origin, writable owner, impersonation 409 and uuid-or-404; `/api/operator` joins `isProtectedOwnerApiRoute` (pass H1-A); one `hasImpersonationCookie` in `impersonation/cookie.ts` replaces two byte-identical copies (H1-B); the conversation read gains `?locationId=`, a nullable conversation (empty view), `connectionStatus` and per-turn `unsupported`. The Operator screen at `/dashboard/operator` (Console/Harbor; server page `notFound()` when the flag is off; URL-state polling with an independent 4-minute deadline; cards rendered from stored rows; Refresh plan; six-tab mobile bar gated by `operatorEnabled`). No migration, no flag flip, no new dependency; nothing posts. Plan hardened over three Codex rounds (11 + 7 + 1 findings, all accepted); two surface amendments (the page path, and this file for the ledger line). The counter is the next pass's business.

- 2026-09-03 — `perf(#1082)` (#1111): `settleGenerationExhaustion` batches its
  per-hole **Refusal** writes into one `writeRefusalEvents` statement, inside
  the transaction that already holds the per-Business advisory lock and the
  `solicitation_batches` row lock. Touches
  `src/lib/solicitation/ask-generation.ts` ·
  `src/lib/solicitation/__tests__/{ask-generation.test.ts,ask-generation.integration.test.ts}`.
  **COUNTS** — a production file under `src/lib/`. Takes the counter to **14**.
  **27 executable production lines; no new statement, no schema, no
  dependency.** The row for this merge is at the bottom of this file and
  carries eight lettered notes; the two worth surfacing here: **(f)** the
  ticket's own bound was wrong — `holes` is capped at `BATCH_CAP` (25), not
  `MAX_DATA_ROWS` (5,000), a constant borrowed from the neighbouring
  `ask_withheld` lane; and the correction then produced a SECOND error, the
  inference that 25 made the ticket marginal. The staging drill measured it:
  **1754 ms for 25 single-row inserts against 141 ms for one set insert**, so
  ~1.6 s of lock-held time. A round trip costs ~70 ms through PgBouncer to
  Supabase and ~0.4 ms to a local Docker Postgres, which is why the integration
  suite's own timings understate the production saving by two orders of
  magnitude. **A ROW COUNT IS NOT A COST.** Evidence:
  `docs/runbooks/evidence/2026-09-03-1082-exhaustion-refusal-batch-drill.md`.

- 2026-09-03 — `feat(#1086)` (map #1084, build #1094): the dashboard gets its
  first tooltip primitive, `src/components/ui/InfoTip.tsx`, and five tips across
  four surfaces. Touches `src/components/ui/InfoTip.tsx` ·
  `src/components/dashboard/kpi/{ReputationHeroBand,RepPulseCard,WeeklyRecapCard}.tsx` ·
  `src/components/dashboard/NeedsAttentionList.tsx` ·
  `src/app/(authenticated)/_styles/console.css`. Takes the counter to **15**.
  **It does NOT reuse `useMenuPopover`**, which `docs/brand-asset-library/design-systems/console/DESIGN.md`
  names the single owner of popover behavior — that hook `preventDefault`s
  Arrow/Home/End *before* checking whether a `menuitem` exists, so a tip, which
  has none, would swallow arrow-key page scrolling; reuse would also be the
  first `components/ui/` → `components/dashboard/` import in the repo. The rule
  is written about **menus** (`aria-haspopup="menu"`); a tip is a disclosure. A
  test named `does not swallow arrow keys while it is open` pins the reason so
  it cannot rot. **Two of the six planned surfaces deliberately carry no tip**
  under the ticket's own rule that a tip is warranted only when a better label
  will not do — `PendingApprovalsStrip` already explains itself in its sub-line,
  and `AgentTeamStrip` has a second reason: the one unstated fact is whether an
  agent posts unattended, which depends on subscription tier and autonomy flags
  the component never receives, so a static tip could only be true for one tier.
  Both omissions are pinned by their own test. **Hover is bound only where
  `(hover: hover)` reports a real pointer** — a tap emits a synthetic
  `mouseenter` on many touch browsers, and opening on it would let the click
  that follows toggle the tip straight shut. `allowed_edit_surface` was amended
  **once**, with owner approval, to admit six existing dashboard test files; the
  intake promised surface tests but locked their files out.

- 2026-09-04 — `refactor(#1086 follow-up)`: the shared half of Console popover
  behaviour gets one owner, `src/components/ui/useDisclosurePopover.ts`. Touches
  that new file · `src/components/dashboard/shell/useMenuPopover.ts` ·
  `src/components/ui/InfoTip.tsx` · `docs/brand-asset-library/design-systems/console/DESIGN.md`. Takes the
  counter to **16**. This closes the follow-up #1086 recorded in its deviations
  D7 and rejected as M3 at the time: the review was right that the dismissal
  machinery was duplicated, and the owner was right that #1086 was the wrong
  branch to fix it on. **`useMenuPopover`'s public API is byte-identical**, so
  `AccountMenu` and `LocationPicker` are untouched — including its ids, which is
  why the core takes a `panelIdSegment` and the menu passes `'menu'`. **The
  split is load-bearing:** `onMenuKeyDown` `preventDefault`s Arrow/Home/End
  before checking whether an item exists, which is correct for a menu and would
  swallow arrow-key page scrolling for a tip, so it stays in the menu layer. One
  deliberate BEHAVIOUR change rides along: outside-dismissal moves from
  `mousedown` to `pointerdown` for the account menu and the location switcher
  too, because iOS Safari synthesises no mouse event for a tap on a plain
  `<div>` — the same defect #1086's review found in the tip. `useMenuPopover`
  keeps its own `toggle` rather than the core's, because opening through
  `openMenu()` is what resets the focus target; sharing the core's would make a
  menu once opened with ArrowDown keep landing on the first item.

Merges since the 2026-08-24 pass — **CLOSED by the 2026-08-26 pass.** Retained
verbatim as record; these are the 8 bullets that stood against a measured 12.

- 2026-08-26 — `fix-967-post-purge-global-optout` (#975): the Solicitation
  lane's unmatched-attribution arm. A suppression-bearing Resend event whose
  `solicitation_outreach` row is gone now writes the recipient's GLOBAL opt-out
  from the SIGNED payload with `accountId: null` and acks, instead of throwing
  into a retry that could never succeed. Full detail in the table row at the
  bottom of this file. **Three notes for the next pass, none of them about the
  fix itself:** (a) the change removed these events from
  `checkFailedInboundEvents`' `status='failed'` set — **silently retiring an
  operator pager, which no gate can detect** and which only the step-7 review
  caught; the replacement is a `captureMessage` at the source, and the general
  rule is now in lab-notes. (b) The `reclassify` WARN fired at 211 vs 90
  estimated production lines while the work had NOT sprawled — 6 of 6 declared
  files, one seam, ~64 executable lines, 55% of the additions comment in a file
  already 55% comment. **The trigger measures a proxy that this repo's comment
  density defeats**; consider splitting the estimate into executable vs comment,
  or the next honest small change will trip it too. (c) `ci:merge-ledger`
  accepts a line in EITHER this bullet list or the bottom table, which is how
  the counter above drifted — see the correction note.

- 2026-08-25 — `feat-landing-mobile-carousel`: phone card rails for landing
  sections 2–5 (owner-directed). New shared `carousel.tsx`/`carousel.css`:
  below 767px the fleet's two tiers, the persona grid, the watchtower grid and
  the committed grid become CSS scroll-snap rails with a dot indicator. The
  four sections went 6,891px → 4,010px at 390px wide, measured. **Desktop is
  untouched by construction** — the rail restyles the container each section
  ALREADY renders rather than adding a wrapper, so no desktop rule moves.
  Four notes for the next pass: (a) the rail selector is 0,2,0 to tie with
  `.rw-lp-fleet-tier[data-tier='watchdog']` and `carousel.css` must stay the
  LAST landing CSS import — a load-bearing ORDER dependency, the second in this
  file after `agent-flip-card.css`'s, and neither is mechanically guarded;
  (b) the slides carry `opacity/transform: !important` because `Staged` gates
  each card on 30% of ITSELF being in view and a peeking card shows ~15% —
  motion writes those inline, so `!important` is the only reach; (c) the dots'
  overflow test measures the SLIDES, never `scrollWidth`, after the committed
  card's `inset: -0.875rem` glow put 570 into `scrollWidth` against a 556
  client width and rendered a hidden dot row into the desktop DOM; (d) adding
  the CSS import pulled `LandingExperience.tsx` into the fallow gate's scope
  and exposed `LandingChrome` at 0% coverage (CRAP 56) — fixed with a real
  test, not a suppression, but it is worth noting that a ONE-LINE import edit
  is enough to surface an inherited complexity finding as a PR blocker.
  4 new files, 13 modified; focus test 17 → 18 controls; landing
  measurements.md + directory.md updated.

- 2026-08-25 — `fix/887-adr-citations`: the no-internal-id posture on the
  Requests surface was cited to **ADR 0012** in four places. ADR 0012 is the
  sentiment-blind withhold enum and says nothing about internal ids; the real
  provenance is the #629 round-2 review, finding F10. A false citation is worse
  than none — it sends the reader to an authority that does not support the
  rule, and the honest conclusion from there is that the rule is unsupported.
  `halt/route.ts` and `approve/route.ts` now cite F10 directly and state that
  they ARE the precedent `regenerate`/`withdraw` cite, so the four route
  comments stop forming a circular reference. Two notes for the next pass:
  (a) the issue named TWO sites and a filename grep found **four** — the extras
  are `approval-routes.test.ts:398` and `docs/directory.md:130`, the latter
  being the interesting one because it conflated two DIFFERENT rules under one
  citation (no star rating, genuinely ADR 0012; no internal id, F10), and the
  half that was right is precisely what made the half that was wrong look
  sound; (b) nothing mechanically ties a comment's ADR reference to that ADR's
  subject, so this class is invisible until someone follows a citation.
  Comment and docs only — no behaviour change, no test change.

- 2026-08-25 — `fix/892-purge-sentinels`: the account purge's tombstone writer
  and its verifier agreed because two humans typed the same strings twice each
  — the 2026-08-18 pass's W7. New `src/lib/account-offboarding/purge-sentinels.ts`
  owns them; both sides compose their own predicate from shared VALUES, keeping
  the writer's `=` and the verifier's `IS DISTINCT FROM` visible at the call
  sites. No shared SQL fragment builder (it would need an `Sql` param and hide
  that asymmetry), and the `SET` clause and `OR`-chain stay two lists.
  **Four notes for the next pass, three of them corrections to the issue:**
  (a) the issue tallies SEVEN duplicated pairs; there are **eight** —
  `accounts.flags = '{}'::jsonb` is absent from its table while its own count
  discussion names `flags` as a restored default. Eight pairs, nine members
  (the email is one pair in two fragments). (b) the operator standardisation
  covers **nine** sentinel comparisons, eight converting — including the two
  `NOT LIKE` sites, which carry no `%`, `_` or backslash and were therefore
  equality checks written as pattern matches. The rule is not stylistic: this
  gate is fail-closed and its predicates sit in an `OR`-chain, where a
  three-valued predicate reads as "not a violation" and OPENS the gate, so `<>`
  made correctness depend on today's `NOT NULL` constraints. (c) the issue
  names two places stating the count wrongly; there are **four** — plus
  `purge-verification.ts`'s `archived_at` comment ("the only NON-residue
  predicate"; it is the only PRESENCE check) and `directory.md`'s claim to
  cover "every column the tombstone `UPDATE` writes", which is false because
  `updated_at` is written and deliberately unverified. (d) **the counts are now
  PINNED** — 28 predicates and their 5/1/22 split in `purge-verification.test.ts`,
  29 writer assignments in `PurgeWorker.test.ts`, both mutation-verified. This
  issue exists because 27 rotted unpinned, and the first cut of the fix
  replaced it with four fresh unpinned numbers; lab-notes "An exact count
  written into a comment is a maintenance trap" caught it in review.
  Runtime coverage cannot catch a missed operator conversion — every column but
  `tone` is `NOT NULL`, so both operators behave identically against a live
  database — so the two new properties read the SQL the module EMITS, not its
  source text. Prerequisite: the suite's `mockSql` was a bare `vi.fn`, so
  introducing `exec.json` crashed 8 tests before they could fail informatively.

- 2026-08-25 — `fix/895-truth-tripwires`: five independent fixes from the
  2026-08-18 pass's H6 bundle — (a) the admin batch panel read
  `HALT_REASON_LABELS[haltReason]` unguarded, and #917's `platform_halt` made
  the **Halt reason** union six-wide, so a browser cached behind that deploy

  still claimed `recordSuppression` had zero production callers; (d) the

  where its four siblings carry it; (f) `auth-island.css` redeclared 8 Island
  colours and 2 brand fonts, the fonts already diverged — this pass's own X10
  finding. (b) shipped in #920/#900 and was not touched.
  **Three notes for the next pass, all about the issue text being wrong:**
  (i) #895(d) is wrong TWICE. Its "moving `agent_runs` earlier inverts an order
  three other files depend on" is over-broad — `runContentPurgeStage` takes both
  per-business advisory locks ~110 lines ABOVE the delete, so any position after
  the loop preserves `advisory → batches row → agent_runs`. AND the rationale
  already in the file — "deleting runs first would only orphan them" — is
  FALSE: the outcomes delete is scoped by `account_id`, not `agent_run_id`, so
  runs-first would merely NULL the FK (071:71) and still remove every row.
  `PurgeWorker.test.ts` pinned that non-constraint with an index comparison
  while the REAL constraint went unguarded; the assertion is now
  lock-before-runs and the mock returns a business row so the loop executes.
  (ii) #895(c)'s "Resend retries forever" is refuted by the lane's own
  docstring — `verifySignature` 401s every attempt past the 300s window before
  lane dispatch. The real residual is that a post-purge bounce cannot write the
  global row to the **Suppression ledger**, because `loadAttribution` reads the
  **Normalized handle** from our own contact row and the purge deletes it.
  Filed as **#967** (go-live blocker) rather than patched: the obvious fix
  contradicts a tested invariant that the lane never normalizes the
  provider-echoed recipient. (iii) the contract now points at
  `recordSuppression` as the one chokepoint instead of enumerating four call
  sites — a hand-maintained census is the drift that went stale here.
  9 files; four new assertions, each mutation-verified RED before trusting it.

- 2026-08-24 — `fix-flip-card-touch-latch` (#955): landing flip-card input fix.
  The agent card's `:hover` flip arm is gated to `(hover: hover) and (pointer:
  fine)` — sticky tap-hover latched a flipped card on every touch device: the
  tap that set `data-flipped` back to false left `:hover` holding the card
  flipped, so a second tap could never restore the front. The click toggle is
  gated to non-hover devices for the mirror reason (a desktop click latched
  the card flipped after the pointer left). 2 production files
  (`agent-flip-card.{css,tsx}`); flip-selector contract lines updated in
  landing DESIGN.md + measurements.md.

> **The counter was CORRECT this time — the first pass in five for which that is

> four-pass streak of hand-corrections — 2026-08-10 (3 missed), -08-15 (#803
> invisible), -08-18 (read 8, measured 11), -08-22 (four missed, incl. #905 at 51
> files) — is **closed**. Do not re-derive that streak next pass; verify the
> current window and move on.

> **Corrected 2026-08-23 from a stale 3.** The counter had missed four merges.
> Two were never counted at the time (#901, #905); two are the #917/#923 pair
> that landed on 2026-08-22 with no increment. The derivation below is written
> out so the next reader can check it instead of trusting it — **this is the
> FOURTH consecutive pass to find this counter wrong by its own rule**, after
> 2026-08-10 (3 missed increments), 2026-08-15 (PR #803 invisible) and
> 2026-08-18 (read 8, measured 11). *Corrected from "THIRD" by the 2026-08-22
> pass (P1): the log's own recurrence counter had the same defect as the thing
> it counts.* Issue **#896** is the mechanical fix; **its Part 1 has now
> landed** as `ci:merge-ledger`, so a merge that changes production or
> governance files and leaves no line here fails CI on the merging PR.
>
> **Rule applied:** a first-parent merge counts when it changes production code
> under `src/`, `worker/` or `supabase/`. Docs-only merges and post-merge sweeps
> do not count. A test-only merge does not count.
>

- **Architecture pass — 2026-08-24** (windowed: `42a247b1..0278366c`; 13
  first-parent merges, **11 counting**, 75 production files). Artifacts:
  `docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-08-24/`. **Counter reset 11 → 0.**

  **Window shape:** one merge is 64% of it — #950/#631, the Solicitation
  auto-approval lane (48 files, migration 113), landed hours before the pass.
  The rest is the admin consolidation wave (#880/#886/#893/#909, 19 files) and
  the send-lane vocabulary work (#890/#891/#913/#914). Excluded: #948
  (governance-only) and #942 (test-only).

  **Health baseline: 20/20 CI audits, `typecheck` and `lint` all green.**
  Migration lockstep verified by direct `pg_indexes` introspection in **both**
  projects — 112 and 113 fully applied, no drift. The standing 078-absent
  exception was re-introspected in production and is **accurate, not stale**
  (`businesses.subscription_tier` nullable; neither check constraint present).

  **Headline — the notice-family clone, decided.** The family has **six**
  members, not the four the seed claimed (`autonomy-suspended-notice` was
  omitted; `worker/jobs/notification/` is the progenitor). Decision: **bless the
  four-file shape, extract the invariants.** A shared `runNoticeJob` owner is
  explicitly REJECTED — every current member assumes a single `businessId`, and
  the next plausible member (Insights / Governor) needs account-scoped fan-in, so
  extracting today encodes the wrong axis. Three latent bugs, all of the form
  "the fix landed in some copies":
  - **BUG 1** `auto-post-notice` enrolls in `OWNER_DIRECTED_TEMPLATES`
    (`resend.ts:97-99`), so a bounce **sets** `owner_email_bounced` — but it is
    the only member of six that never **reads** it (0 references vs 7–8 in the
    other five). Identified 2026-07-12 as "known follow-up #3a";
    `grep -rn "#3a" docs/` returns one hit, the sentence itself. Six weeks live,
    untracked.
  - **BUG 2** `auto-post-notice` and `escalation-notice` lack the 15-minute
    stale-claim reap the other three have **and** read a claim conflict as a
    harmless dedup. One crash dedup-blocks a channel permanently while the outbox
    reports success — losing the Guardian persona's only owner-facing output.
  - **BUG 3** three members run the provider error classifier on Postgres errors
    and can mark a delivered notice failed. The two newest members carry a
    written warning about exactly this.

  **Second finding — the autonomy control surface is split.** Two independent
  kill-switch families now exist; `AUTO_APPROVAL_ENABLED` has **zero** effect on
  the Solicitation lane. Scoped honestly: `env-vars.md:661` is correct per-flag,
  invariant #3 is **not** violated (that lane does not post to Google), and
  production has none of the flags set — **zero live hazard**. The gap is that
  the runbooks enumerating "the autonomy flags" predate this lane. **Must close
  before #633 flips anything.**

  **Seed 3 REFUTED — a clean result, recorded as such:** the four admin
  consolidations compose cleanly. No file imports from more than one of the four
  seams; three stack in disjoint layers and the fourth (`#886`) is on the owner
  surface, unreachable from any admin route.

  **Prior handoffs: five of nine genuinely closed** (#890, #893, #896, and both
  08-22 next-session PRs). #891 is closed but incompletely adopted — **latent,
  not a live defect**; a first reading called it live and the correction plus its

  #892, #894 and #895 are untouched across two consecutive windows and

  **Five DO-NOT-UNIFY entries added**, including a boundary correction: the

- **feat — `/feature` run progress + the Claude/Codex review record** (2026-08-23).
  **Governance-only: this merge changes no file under `src/`, `worker/` or
  `supabase/`, so by this log's own rule it does NOT increment the counter,
  which stays at 10.** It lands here because `.agents/skills/` is on the
  `ci:merge-ledger` governance surface — which is the point of that audit: the
  #905 blind spot was merges that leave no trace, not merges that count.
  Six new files under `.agents/skills/feature/`, three edited. A run now prints
  an 8-step ladder at every phase boundary and on resume, and serves a
  loopback-only page carrying that ladder, a findings ledger with ACCEPT/REJECT
  verdicts, and the Claude/Codex review stream; `snapshot` writes the same page
  as an Artifact fragment for a shareable record. **Ownership is the design:**
  `progress.json` holds only the ladder and tracers — `state.json` keeps phase,
  branch, SHAs and review counters, and the gate files keep gate truth, so the
  new file duplicates nothing (pinned by a gate that fails on any `state.json`
  key appearing in `progress.json`). Codex output is never copied: the
  transcript stores a POINTER to the companion's job files, leaving the
  companion the single owner of its verdict, findings and log.
  `lib/feature-snapshot.mjs` is the one merge point for all three readers.
  **Verified under a 31-gate ledger against a real adversarial-review job,
  which found four defects in this code before it landed** — a self-carry guard
  that missed symlinked paths (`resolve` does not follow symlinks while git
  returns the root resolved), a failed `start` that orphaned a listener `stop`
  could never reach, a stale registry that could SIGTERM an unrelated process
  after pid reuse, and a cross-round triage that published a REJECTED finding
  as ACCEPTED. Two of the sweep's own tests were also wrong and are recorded
  because a test that lies is worse than none: a `die` inside a subshell let a
  case print OK with three assertions failed, and the docs-coverage gate probed
  commands by EXECUTING them, so it started real servers on port 7717.
  **Known collision, not resolved here:** `codex/feature-workflow-hardening` is
  a parallel, almost entirely uncommitted hardening of this same skill
  (`tracer-ledger.mjs`, `review-findings.mjs`, `durable-store.mjs`) that would
  give tracers, findings and durable reads a second owner. It edits the same
  three documents. Someone must choose before it lands.

- **refactor #880** — `useAdminActionForm`, and all five admin surfaces migrated
  in one change (2026-08-23; surfaced by #865's branch review, standards axis).
  The `useReducer` + 7-to-9-member action union + switch + `initialState` was
  copied across `ImpersonationStartClient`, `CsvImportClient`,
  `ManualRankingSection`, `PastDueExtensionPanel` and `SolicitationBatchPanel` —
  the five `rg 'submitStart' src/app/admin` returns, matching Pathfinder X7's
  count rather than a guess. **X7's "blocking design decision" was understated
  and is now resolved rather than dodged.** X7 records it as `success: boolean`
  vs `message: string | null`; measured across all five the success channel is
  FOUR things — a boolean flag, a message string (×2), *nothing at all*
  (impersonation navigates away), and a whole `CsvImportReport`. So it is a TYPE
  PARAMETER, not a unified field: `TOutcome` defaults to `never`, which makes
  `succeed` uncallable on the form that has no success, checked by the compiler
  instead of by a comment. Flattening all four to `string | null` would have
  cost the CSV report its structure and given the impersonation form a channel
  it must never populate. **Two things deliberately stay in the surfaces**: the
  fetch (five bodies, five response shapes, five failure sentences — a hook that
  owned them needs a config object per surface, the knobbed-helper shape this
  repo rejects), and `CsvImportClient`'s SECOND error channel
  (`validationErrors`), which is one surface's affordance and would otherwise be
  an array four surfaces never set. Ordering is preserved exactly: `clearOutcome`
  before validating so a refusal shows while the button still reads its idle
  label, and `stop` clearing only `busy` so a `fail` raised inside the body
  survives the `finally`. 16 hook cases; 348 admin tests green and UNTOUCHED.
  **Counts: +1 production merge.**
- **refactor #886** — `ApprovalPanel.tsx`'s lane-write seam extracted to a
  sibling `lane-writes.ts` (2026-08-23; deferred from the #872 branch review,
  standards finding #1 — the only *major* finding on that branch, rejected as a
  blocker THERE and accepted as a follow-up). Unblocked because #865 and #872
  both landed: nothing else edits the file, and there is no locked
  `allowed_edit_surface` to widen. **1,123 → 703 lines**, back under the 1,000
  the thermo-nuclear review treats as a presumptive blocker. What moved is the
  whole `── The write ──` section that already read as a seam — `LaneStatus`,
  `LANE_REFUSAL_COPY`, `NOT_REGENERABLE_COPY`, `postLaneAction`, the four
  `Announce` sentences and `useLaneWrites` (which now owns FOUR writes, and that
  is what pushed the section over). `LaneWriteOutcome`/`LaneRefresh` are exported
  from the new module rather than the panel, and `RequestsClient` imports them
  from there — they are the write's vocabulary, not the panel's. `countPhrase`
  moved with them: the panel's prose reads it too, and leaving it behind would
  make the new module import from its own importer. **The line falls where it
  does on purpose**: the panel's other three hooks (`useExcludedAsks`,
  `usePendingRegeneration`, `useArmedWithdrawal`) are about what is on SCREEN
  and stay; the write seam is about what is on the WIRE. The docblock's "one
  file-local hook each" claim was corrected rather than left true-ish — three
  are file-local, one is not, and the reason is written down. **Behaviour frozen
  and proven so: the requests `__tests__` directory is 83 green, UNTOUCHED** (a
  test needing an edit would have been evidence the move changed behaviour).
  `docs/directory.md` gains the row in the same PR. **Counts: +1 production
- **refactor #909** — the admin gate→parse→400 preamble extracted, AND
  `ci:admin-aal2` widened by one gate shape, in one PR (2026-08-23; follow-up
  B3.11 from #898). `requireAdminJson(request, schema)` composes
  `requireAdminWithAal2` inside `src/lib/auth/admin-mfa-gate.ts` — no new file —
  and `admin/autonomy/unsuspend`, `admin/solicitation/batch/halt` and
  `admin/solicitation/breaker/reset` now call it. **The audit change is the half
  that makes this legitimate**: `GATE_JSON_FN` is a THIRD recognized gate shape,
  credited only when bound by an import that RESOLVES to the canonical module,
  with the fail-closed posture unchanged — a different wrapper from the same
  module is still a finding. **Branch review then found the provenance check
  itself was weaker than its own header claimed**: it matched the trailing path
  segment, so `@/evil/admin-mfa-gate` was credited and any file could mint gate
  credit by choosing a filename. `resolveSpecifier` now maps the `@/` alias onto
  `src/` and resolves a relative specifier against the importing file. Two of the
  audit's OWN fixtures had been spelling relative depths that do not resolve and
  passing on the basename alone — the loose check was already costing accuracy
  before anyone abused it. The identical extraction was built and reverted during #898
  step 8 for want of exactly this half; **the revert was correct**, and the
  lesson recorded in lab-notes [2026-08-21] stands for the next preamble. The
  `.fallowrc.jsonc` acknowledgement is removed and `ci:fallow` is green without
  it (8 changed files, 0 duplication findings). 9 new audit cases pin both
  directions of the widening; 7 new helper cases pin that both 400 shapes and
  the gate's own 401/403/500 arms are byte-identical to what the three routes
  spelled by hand. No route test needed editing — they mock the auth PRIMITIVES,
  which is the shape lab-notes [2026-08-21] prescribed after the first attempt.
  **Counts: +1 production merge.**
- **fix #893** — the admin read fan-out bounded, and the enumerated pins replaced
  by a rule (2026-08-23). **Part 1 grew from two subjects to four**: the ticket
  named `lookupSuppressions` (`suppressions-service.ts:72`) and
  `getAdminNotifications` (`notifications-service.ts:137`); this change also found
  the cost-report rollup branch (`cost-report-service.ts:321`, two templates) and
  `processRow` (`csv-import.ts:301`, THREE reads built from helper calls, so
  nothing in the expression mentioned `sql` twice). All four now sequence; the four
  services' own suites are green untouched, so the fix changed no behaviour.
  **Part 2** is `ci:pool-fanout` (`scripts/ci/pool-fanout-audit.ts`, wired into
  ci.yml's `lint` job and `lefthook.yml`): an AST rule over
  `src/lib/{admin,dashboard,locations}` failing any `Promise` combinator with ≥2
  DB-issuing elements, counting tagged templates, `sql.unsafe(...)` **and calls
  that pass a `Sql` binding** — the third form is what a tagged-template-only rule
  would have missed on subjects B and D. Fail-closed on an unreadable argument;
  the escape is a reasoned per-site pragma with zero uses today. **The four
  existing pins were deliberately NOT widened, and no per-service pin was added** —
  four fixes plus four new pins would rebuild the enumeration one directory
  further apart. **Branch review then found the classifier was ITSELF an
  enumeration, one level down**: it matched a hand-listed set of top-level
  shapes, so a logical operand, a query wrapped in a call, and a query inside a
  thunk each counted ONE and passed. It is now a SUBTREE scan — find the query
  wherever it is — and the thunk carve-out is gone, because `settledInOrder` is
  not a `Promise` combinator and never reached the scan anyway. Three boundaries
  are stated in the audit header rather than implied: the root excludes
  `src/app/**`; the deferred-await form (`const a = getA(sql); const b =
  getB(sql);`) is not covered, because the syntactic proxy for it collides with
  the legitimate fragment builders; and the fail-closed cost is ACCEPTED
  explicitly — a `Promise.all(tasks)` with no DB work in it is reported too,
  because the audit cannot tell it from an unbounded `.map` fan-out. The
  ticket's named `src/app/api/admin/jobs/route.ts:27` exception is a recorded
  DEVIATION, not an omission: that file is outside the root and is one DB call
  plus one Redis call, so the ≥2 rule cannot reach it. **Counts: +1 production
  merge.**

  three route-level `no_send_parent → closed:false` pins. **Counts: +1 production merge.**

- **PR #935** — #896 part 1, `ci:merge-ledger` (2026-08-23). The audit this very row
  satisfies: a PR whose diff changes a non-test file under `src/`/`worker/`/`supabase/`,
  or any path on the governance surface, must add a line here or fail CI on the merging
  PR. **No migration, no flag, no env var, no new dependency.** One script
  (`scripts/ci/merge-ledger-audit.ts`) + 44 unit cases, wired into `package.json`,
  `ci.yml`'s `lint` job and `lefthook.yml`; Dependabot is exempted at the workflow level
  because `package.json` is on the surface and no bot can author a row.
  **Does NOT increment the counter** — it changes no production file, and the audit
  deliberately never touches that number: which merges "count" is the pass's judgement,
  and an audit asserting it on every PR would assert it on the docs-only merges the rule
  excludes. What it enforces is the TRACE.

- **2026-08-22 architecture pass** — windowed on the 17 merges after `54e4d374`
  (7 counting, **77 production files**). 8 features charted, **12 mutations
  executed**, counter reset 7 → 0. Artifacts:
  `docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-08-22/`.

  **Health held for a fifth pass**: 77 B, duplication 1.1%, high-complexity
  functions 122 → 119, churn hotspots 0, LOC +4.3% without added tangle. The one
  metric moving the wrong way is large functions (449 → 463) against
  maintainability 92.2 → 92.1.

  **What it found that the code did not already know.** (1) The send lane's
  *decision* logic is well pinned and its **concurrency and integrity spine is
  not**: the A4 bytes-identity check and BOTH send-path CAS predicates survived
  deletion with the whole lane green, and a whole-repo grep of `src` + `worker`
  returned zero test-file references for `bytes_changed_under_gate`, `cas_lost`
  and `in_flight_elsewhere`. All three failure modes are silent — moderating one
  artifact and sending another, a value-ledger row with no join key behind it,
  and a double send. The pass's own priority hypothesis (guard 5,
  `readSendBreaker`'s fail-closed throw) was **refuted**: it is pinned three
  ways. (2) **X18 — `canonicalBatchId`'s bug is reproduced.**
  `settleGenerationExhaustion` binds one batch id cast-and-case-insensitive and
  raw-TEXT inside ONE transaction, so it locks the batch and misses its send
  parent; the canonicaliser exists precisely because this already shipped once
  in `approval.ts`. (3) **P1 — the counter was wrong for the FOURTH consecutive
  pass**, and this log's own recurrence line said THIRD. (4) **`!== 'not_settled'`
  is a wrong proxy**: halting an all-withheld `built` batch makes both halt routes
  report `closed: true` against `send_closed_at IS NULL`, because
  `'no_send_parent' !== 'not_settled'` is true and that arm is reachable through
  `haltBatch` → `latchHalt`, which passes no failure. (5) **`ci:admin-aal2`'s P2
  parity misses `/admin/solicitation`** — it anchors on a sibling route that does
  not exist for that page, so flipping the page's `aal2` off would pass CI while
  the API route stays gated, and that page reads `solicitation_send_breaker`
  directly via `sql`. (6) **`ci:rls-audit` guards no platform-scoped table** —
  `REQUIRED_RLS_TABLES = TENANT_TABLES`, so neither `solicitation_send_breaker`
  nor four older tables are covered.

  **Two claims in this log were measured wrong.** The **#920 entry below still
  says *"+4 platform-wide predicates under a standing sign-off"*, and that is
  false**: the move is **381 → 383 (#904) → 390 (#920)**, split across two
  merges, seven of them from #920, and all seven are `background-global-scan` —
  only #904's two are `platform-cross-account`. The allowlist's own header
  narrative records all nine correctly; **this log is the artefact at fault.**
  Second: **`#895(b)` was delivered by #920** and `approval.ts:518-524` cites the
  issue by number, while the issue stayed open — the tracker and the source
  disagree about whether it is done.

  **X10 graduated from pattern to rule** — an enumeration that defines a guard's
  SUBJECT SET must be derived from the artefact that produces subjects; an
  enumeration listing only EXEMPTIONS inside a derived scan is legitimate. Seven
  silent instances against ten-plus loud ones, plus one (`SQL_TAG_NAMES`) that
  made the transition in-repo. Phase 2 amended it: apply the classifier to a
  guard's **discriminator and its transitive inputs**, which found two more.

  **The backlog is ONE ranked list of 15 items** (the seven re-verified
  2026-08-18 handoffs merged with this pass's findings, ranked on expected cost
  of not doing it before the next window). Top three: **H8** the three
  send-path integrity tests, **#896** `ci:merge-ledger` + the Stop-hook
  sentinel — the only item that changes the *rate* at which everything else
  accumulates — and **H9** `canonicalBatchId` at `ask-generation.ts`'s two
  entry points.

  **What the next pass should look at.** (a) Whether the three send-path
  integrity tests still go RED under their own mutations — #932 pinned them, and
  a later refactor can silently unpin them again. (b) **`ci:fallow:coverage` has
  no unit-size axis**: `line_count` never appears in any finding's `exceeded`
  field, so a 202-line flat orchestrator with high coverage is invisible to the
  PR gate in any file, forever. (c) Whether `#896`'s ledger actually changed the
  counter's accuracy — three hand-corrections of a hand-kept number is not a
  process that has converged, and this entry is the first one written under it.
  (d) The four false `docs/directory.md` rows, and whether H11's split of
  `readSendBreaker` retired two of them structurally rather than by prose.

  *This entry was re-derived from the pass's artifacts on 2026-08-23 after the
  original local edit was lost before it was committed. The four blocks above
  marked with the #920 correction, X10's rule, findings (4)-(6) and the
  next-pass list were restored from the original text on the same day. The artifacts under
  `docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-08-22/` are authoritative wherever they and this
  summary disagree.*

- **PR #926** — #917 `platform_halt`, the third system **Halt latch** reason (2026-08-22, squash
  `2c7bb615`). **Migration 111** — two CHECK widenings, no data change, applied to staging and
  production and confirmed by introspection 2026-08-23 (three-database hash
  `00cb1b60044b44c408553999433ee2d2`). **No new dependency, no new flag, no new env var.**
  Closes amendment B14's gap by taking its SECOND option: the expiry sweep still runs on the same
  72h clock and the "≤10 days by construction" bound is untouched — only the reason column
  changes. **New single owner:** `resolveWindowHaltReason` decides once per tick which of two
  reasons the sweep writes. **The one architectural fact worth carrying forward:** it introduces
  the FIRST sanctioned `try/catch` around `readSendBreaker`, whose own docstring forbids exactly
  that. The carve-out holds because the operative half of that contract is "and proceeds to send",
  and this read only picks a label — the sending gate in the release phase keeps its throw. That
  is a narrow exception resting on a distinction a future reader can easily miss, so
  `CONTEXT.md`'s **Halt latch** entry and the amendments doc both name it. **What the next pass
  should look at:** whether "label-only read" becomes a pattern with a second instance, at which
  point the distinction needs a mechanical guard rather than three prose statements.
- **PR #925** — #923 the §7.1 refusal, recorded not implemented (2026-08-22, squash `1a8443ce`).
  **No migration, no dependency, no flag.** One production file changed, and only its docstring:
  the substance is **ADR 0022** plus `dispatch-rate-bound.test.ts`, which pins four dials and
  re-derives the solicitation lane's share of Resend's 10 req/s TEAM limit rather than storing it.
  **This is the log's first entry for a merge whose product is a decision**, which is why it
  counts: it spends a spec obligation deliberately and leaves a tripwire so the spending is
  visible. **Known bound, recorded honestly:** the exact-value pin (`toBe(2)`) is the real guard;
  the 1% share ceiling only bites at a per-tick budget of 7 or more, so budgets 3–6 pass the
  arithmetic and are caught by the pin alone.

  **New single owners:** `runReleaseCycle` for the five-phase order; `latchSystemHalt` as the
