Incremented to 2 by **#912** (#899 solicitation send worker, 2026-08-22) — below the
≥ 5 threshold, so no pass is due. **#911** merged the same day and is deliberately NOT
counted: it changed two test fixtures and zero production lines, and this log's own
window measure is "first-parent commits **with production lines**". Recorded here rather
than left implicit, because the 2026-08-18 pass's headline finding was that the counter
had been wrong by its own rule and nobody had checked.

**That pass measured the counter itself and found it wrong by its own rule.** The
window held **32 first-parent commits, 11 with production lines**; the counter read
**8**. #857, #866 and #875 got no post-merge sweep at all, and **nine further merges
rewrote the enforcement surface with zero trace in this file** — including #849,
which deleted `.github/workflows/fallow.yml` and rewrote `ci-audit-wiring.test.ts`,
the test that exists to stop that drift. The 2026-08-15 pass framed PR #803 as an
anomaly; it is the norm. Handoff **H7** proposes the fix (`ci:merge-ledger`, two
triggers, run on the MERGING PR because "the sweep did not run" is the failure mode).

Superseded counter text, retained for the record: the pre-pass line read *"8 — PAST
THE ≥ 5 THRESHOLD"*, with a caveat that #857 and #866 might make it 10. The measured
figure is 11. Reset by
the **2026-08-15 pass**
(table row below). Full artifacts: `docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-08-15/`. The
window's merge list, retained for the record: #793, #805, #821, #827, #831, #833
(entries below). **Not counted, and correctly so** — #830 (marketing copy),
#822/#819/#817 (docs). **Logged by that pass as a workflow-surface change the
product counter is structurally blind to: PR #803** (`0e1a5d41`, *"chore:
optimize Codex agent configuration"*) rewrote `.agents/skills/feature/SKILL.md`
from 667 to 207 lines and **deleted step 8 outright** — the surface three
separate architecture-pass prescriptions were aimed at. See
`docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-08-15/05-process-findings.md` §3.

**Handoff-execution PRs are NOT counted (policy, made explicit 2026-08-16).**
The six PRs that executed this pass's handoffs — **#836** (H1), **#837** (H3),
**#840** (H6), **#838** (H2), **#839** (H4), **#841** (H5), merged in that order —
do not increment the counter. A pass's own prescriptions landing cannot be the
entropy that triggers the next pass, or every pass becomes self-triggering; and
these PRs are net entropy-negative by construction (owners consolidated, guards
pinned, one audit hardened). This was already the practice — the 2026-08-10
pass's #785/#787/#788/#815/#816 were likewise never counted — but it was never
written down. It applies **only** to PRs whose entire content is a recorded
handoff from `04-handoff-prompts.md`; a handoff PR that grows unrelated scope
counts as a normal merge. Landing record: `docs/reviews/PATHFINDER-2026-08-15/`
+ each PR body.

- **PR #904** — #898 **Send governors**: the schema and three governors #899/#900
  stand on (2026-08-21, squash `b873a9a5`). **Architecturally: one migration, one
  new TABLE, two new `src/lib/solicitation/` modules, one new admin page + two
  routes, one new `HealthAlertPoller` check — no new dependency, no new env var,
  no new flag, no new top-level directory.** Migration **110** was applied to
  staging **and** production in lockstep on merge day (all three databases
  byte-identical, `3e644c28…`), so it leaves **no standing exception** — the prod
  exception list still names only 078.

  What it adds to the map: **`solicitation_send_breaker` is the first
  platform-scoped table with no `account_id`** — deliberately not a
  `TENANT_TABLES` member and not in `TABLE_DISPOSITIONS`, carrying deny-all RLS
  instead, because the event it records is platform-wide and `account_audit_log`
  is account-scoped. **That table IS its own audit record.** Also: `checks.ts`
  gains its **first WRITER** (every other check reads), isolated in its own
  try/catch so a constraint violation or lock wait cannot discard the nine
  findings collected before it. Three new LOCKED CONTEXT.md terms (**Send
  breaker**, **Send breaker trip**, **Breaker window**), and the **Solicitation
  lane**'s declared write surface widened by exactly one column (`bounce_kind`).

  **What the next pass should look at.** (1) **`ci:admin-aal2` vs `fallow` are in
  direct tension over the admin-route preamble** — this branch built the
  extraction, failed the AAL2 audit on all three routes, and reverted; follow-up
  **B3.11** carries the extraction plus the gate-shape change together, and that
  is an architecture decision, not a lint fix. (2) **Three governors ship with no
  consumer** — the breaker trips and pages but nothing reads it on the send path
  until #900, so the whole surface is inert-by-construction and its first real
  exercise is someone else's branch. (3) **`readBreakerWindow` still seq-scans**
  (follow-up B3.8); 110's `sent_at` index cannot serve an OR-of-three, and the
  migration's own comment claiming a bitmap scan is wrong — 110 is immutable and
  at head, so the correction lives in the deviations file. (4) **B3.9**: the
  level-triggered re-page rides a first-seen Sentry rule, so a multi-day halt
  notifies twice then goes quiet — the alerting half of "an open breaker is a
  STATE" is not actually delivered yet. (5) The **11 follow-ups B3.1–B3.11** are
  the largest single-branch backlog this log has recorded; worth asking whether
  that is healthy decomposition or deferred debt.

- **PR #883** — #872 **Ask withdrawal**: the owner's post-approval recourse at
  ask granularity (2026-08-18, squash `094d71b9`). **Architecturally: one
  migration, one new verb, one new route, one new sibling component — no new
  concept, no new dependency, no new env var or flag, no new top-level
  directory.** Migration **109** widens 108's cancel arm by `owner_withdrawn`
  and was applied to staging **and production** in lockstep on merge day (all
  three databases verified byte-identical), so it leaves **no standing
  exception** — the prod exception list still names only 078. What it adds to
  the map: `withdrawAsk` becomes the **fifth** human-triggered verb in
  `src/lib/solicitation/approval.ts`, and the ADR-0021 decision that it does
  **not** share #865's private `latchHalt` (the latch writes `halted_at` on the
  batch; withdrawal writes nothing there) — same discipline, separate body, with
  a duplicated `delivery_status IN ('rendered','queued')` predicate now in two
  places. **That duplication is the thing for the next pass to look at**: the
  Codex adversarial review flagged it as a drift watch rather than a defect,
  and a third caller would make it a real seam question. Two other standing
  items for the next pass, both recorded with owner-visible reasoning rather
  than fixed here: (a) `ApprovalPanel.tsx` crossed **1,000 lines** (982 →
  1,109) and its pre-existing `── The write ──` section (~415 lines, now four
  lane writes) is the extraction candidate — deferred because the file was
  being edited by #865 concurrently and the new file fell outside the locked
  edit surface; **this is now unblocked and is the first follow-up to file**;
  (b) `WithdrawalDecision` parallels `WithdrawAskResult` by design (a pure,
  plan-locked decision type whose `withdraw` arm is deliberately data-free),
  kept after review. Baselines unmoved: tenant-scope 381, no allowlist entries
  added, `ci:directory-map` complete.

- **PR #882** — #665 purge-verifier blind spots + #662 suppressions-console
  receipt/actor (2026-08-18, squash `fe21d02d`). **Entropy-negative: it removed
  a detector blind spot and a false statement about an audit's reach, adding no
  concept.** No migration, env var, flag, dependency or new file; ~45 production
  lines across three files. **Counted** — it has production lines, which is the
  line #869's *not counted* entry actually draws, not the commit-type prefix
  (cf. `fix(858)`/#868, counted).
  **What changed architecturally.** `purge-verification.ts`'s `businesses`
  residue predicate went from 17 columns to 27 — the five migration-099 postal
  attestation columns the issue named, plus `category`, `tone`,
  `stripe_customer_id`, `stripe_subscription_id` and `archived_at`, which no
  issue had named. The read side now covers **every** column the tombstone
  `UPDATE` in `worker/jobs/account-offboarding/purge-stages.ts` writes. That
  write/read pair is now closed, and closing it is the point: the verifier runs
  *inside* the finalize transaction and **throws**, so it is a gate, not a
  report.
  **New shape worth a pass's attention.** Two predicates are not residue checks
  and read as exceptions to the branch's rule — `tone IS DISTINCT FROM` its
  schema default (the tombstone RESTORES rather than nulls) and
  `archived_at IS NULL` (the tombstone SETS it). A third kind of column in this
  branch would be the signal that the predicate list wants a declarative
  column→expectation table instead of one hand-maintained `OR` chain. It does
  not want one yet at 27.
  **Allowlist baseline unchanged at 381.** The
  `solicitation_suppression_corrections` entry's `sqlSnippet` was re-pinned only
  to stay byte-exact after `lookupSuppressions` gained a `LEFT JOIN users`.
  **Concept corrected, not added:** that entry's comment had claimed the `users`
  join was "structurally scoped and needs no exception". It is not — `users` is
  absent from `TENANT_TABLES`, so `ci:tenant-scope` forms no opinion and no
  exception is *possible*. **The next pass should treat this as a class, not an
  incident:** the audit is silent on every table outside `TENANT_TABLES`, and
  nothing distinguishes "reviewed and exempt" from "never in scope" at a call
  site. A reader who trusts a green audit as a review of a cross-tenant join
  will be wrong again somewhere else.
- **PR #876** — #865 admin **Halt latch** surface (2026-08-18, squash
  `9136d778`). **Entropy-negative where it matters: it gave a dead enum arm its
  writer without adding a concept.** `admin_halt` had been a permitted
  `halt_reason` CHECK member with no code path able to write it, and the
  owner-facing copy for it had already shipped — so this closed a gap rather
  than opening a surface. No migration, no env var, no feature flag, no
  dependency; every value already existed in migrations 091 and 108.
  **New single owner:** `latchHalt` in `src/lib/solicitation/approval.ts` — one
  private body for both halt arms (`haltBatch`, `adminHaltBatch`), owning the
  CAS, the cancellations, the audit row, the spine step and
  `closeBatchIfSettled` in that order. This is the shape the codebase already
  prefers (cf. `closeBatchIfSettled` itself) and it means a third halt writer
  (`send_window_elapsed`/`batch_expired`, still unbuilt, #630) has one obvious
  place to land. **Concept tightened, not added:** both halt verbs now refuse
  `pending`, closing an API-reachable lifecycle hole the intake had explicitly
  deferred — the deferral rested on UI reachability standing in for API
  reachability, which is a reasoning error worth watching for elsewhere.
  **New second read model** on `batch-view.ts` (`readAdminBatchSummary`) whose
  ordering deliberately diverges from `readLatestBatch`'s — two read models over
  one table with different selection policies is a real cost, and it is now
  documented in three places because two separate defects came from that
  divergence being implicit.
  **What the next pass should look at:** (a) `src/lib/solicitation/approval.ts`
  keeps growing — it now holds four public verbs plus the shared latch, and
  #872 will add another; ask whether the halt lane wants its own module.
  (b) The `useReducer` admin-form shape is now duplicated across FIVE admin
  surfaces (#880) — the abstraction is earned and the duplication is the kind a
  pass should force. (c) **Two workflow-infrastructure defects were found by
  this ship, not by the tooling itself** — `verify-gate.sh` racing
  `db-provision` (#877, and it clears on retry, so it self-conceals) and the
  staging deploy target drift (#878). Both are the "surface the product counter
  is structurally blind to" class this log already flagged for PR #803.
  (d) The staging ride caught two defects that three review agents and two
  live-DB suites all missed, because they existed only in the composition of
  write → refetch → render; that is evidence about the *review apparatus*, not
  about this branch.

- **PR #873** — #860 per-ask **Ask regeneration** surface (2026-08-18, squash
  `0f81b9e7`). **Entropy-negative in concept, entropy-positive in one file, and
  it makes a headline product guarantee unconditional.** No migration, env var,
  flag or dependency. Ships **INERT** — `SOLICITATION_SEND_ENABLED` is unset in
  every environment (verified on the Railway staging worker at merge), so the
  route 404s and no control renders; the staging verification was a NEGATIVE one.
  **Concept CLOSED:** #629's deviation D3. `regenerateAsk`'s four-state set
  collapses to the predicate `isRegenerableBatchState(state) => state === 'built'`
  (ADR **0020**, narrowing cross-spec amendment B2), so **Owner approval**'s
  byte-for-byte guarantee loses its conditional in `CONTEXT.md` and in code —
  enforced twice, at the pre-check and at the locked re-check under `FOR UPDATE`.
  **Concept COINED:** **Ask regeneration** (`CONTEXT.md`).
  **`approval.ts` gained a third owner verb** (`requestAskRegeneration`) and is
  now the module the next pass should weigh: three verbs, and the round-1 review
  asked directly whether it was becoming a junk drawer (verdict: still coherent).
  Its shared `lockBatchForAction` now returns `{batch, state}` and is the single
  owner of the lock+derive preamble — the three verbs keep DIFFERENT failure
  expressions on purpose (two return `not_found`, one THROWS, because postgres.js
  commits on a normal return and the enqueue has already written its outbox row).
  **ADR 0020 was amended by its own PR's review** — the Consequences section
  originally priced the post-approval recourse as "a real ergonomic cost"; the
  adversarial axis showed, and the owner verified, that halting never clears
  `solicitation_asks.status='send'`, so `MIN_GAP_DAYS` + `stale_visit` lose the
  whole cohort for that visit. The ADR now says its own pricing "was wrong". The
  cheaper recourse is **#872** (post-approval per-ask withdrawal), filed, not
  built.
  **What the next pass should look at:** (a) `ApprovalPanel.tsx` — ~980 lines,
  a 21-key copy catalogue over four unions; cognitive complexity was taken 16 → 10
  by four extractions, but the file-size question is untouched and was deliberately
  NOT bundled into a gate fix; (b) **`RequestsClient` sits at cognitive 15,
  exactly on fallow's threshold** — pre-existing, untouched, and the next hook or
  JSX conditional added there fails CI; (c) `LaneRefresh`'s naming altitude —
  `replace`/`in_place` name the PARENT's render mechanism and the tell is that
  `Exclude<LaneRefresh, 'none'>` has to exist; a verb-level vocabulary
  (`terminal`/`returns`/`reporting`) would need no `Exclude` (round-2 kept this
  below the findings bar as a rename with no wrong call site); (d) the
  three-axis review's own yield — the **adversarial axis caught a cross-tenant
  409-vs-404 oracle that the spec axis examined and CLEARED**, which is the
  strongest single datapoint this log has for keeping the third axis on
  RISK = high.

  each in `src/lib/notifications/channels/`.

  *structural* to *configuration*. Before, `runAdminChannelStart/Cancel/Disable`
  were admin-only helpers calling `requireAdminWithAal2` unconditionally, so an
  admin route could not skip step-up. Now the runners are portal-agnostic and

  admin routes; cases (m)/(n)/(o)) but its floor is real: an aliased import
  still clears it. The adversarial reviewer's structural remedy (branded actor

  review round**, deliberately and on the record —

> **Answers to three of the next pass's own scope questions, observed while shipping #757 — the pass should start from these rather than re-deriving them.**
>
> - **Scope item (1) — did #757 land P1 as specified? YES, with ONE recorded reversal.** One stack, `kpi/fragments.ts` as single owner (test-pinned), Stack A deleted, `kpi/types.ts` kept. **P1 asked for FOUR fragments; only TWO were extracted** — once Stack A is deleted, `detectedAtWindowFilter` and the TTR validity filter have **zero composers** (Stack B's response rate stays literal per ADR 0019 D2, which P1 itself forbids touching, and Stack B has no duration metric). Owner-adjudicated at intake, recorded as deviation D3 and in ADR 0019. Not drift — but the pass should confirm it agrees.
> - **Scope item (2) — did the sweep-miss mechanization end the missed-increment class? NO: IT NEVER SHIPPED.** `.claude/skills/document-feature/SKILL.md` still does not mention `arch-review-log.md` in its "What to update" table (`grep -c arch-review-log` → 0), so the mandatory log row the pass prescribed does not exist. The class has now failed **three times as prose** (#712, #716, #746) and the fix that was supposed to mechanize it was never applied. This increment was written by hand from the handoff doc's step-8 checklist, not by the skill — i.e. the class is still live and the next miss is a matter of who resumes without that checklist.
> - **Scope items (2) and (4) re-observed while shipping PR #821 — both classes are still live, and both fired again.**
>   - **(2) The mechanization has now SHIPPED — PR #823, 2026-08-14.** `.claude/skills/document-feature/SKILL.md`'s "What to update" table now carries an `arch-review-log.md` row, triggered "Always, on a MERGED feature", specifying the counter increment and the entry shape; the skill also records *why* the row exists so a future editor does not tidy it away. Before that, the class had claimed four features — #712, #716, #746, and #821 (whose increment was written **by hand** from a handoff checklist, because the skill did not ask for it). The prescribed fix survived **two** architecture passes unapplied, most likely because it always fell outside the locked edit surface of whichever feature discovered it: `.claude/skills/**` was outside #821's surface, and that branch had already spent four owner-approved amendments. It took a separate docs PR to land — which is the transferable lesson. **The next pass should verify the row is still present and that increments since #821 were made by the skill rather than by hand.**
>   - **(4) The gate's phase rewind fired three more times.** Same mechanism: `verify-gate.sh` calls `set-phase gate_green` on success unconditionally, so every gate run after review completion rewinds `reviewed` → `gate_green`. During #821's step 7 it fired after the review-fix gate, after the D-13 verification gate, and after the doc-sweep gate; each was repaired by hand with `set-phase reviewed`. Running total: **seven occurrences across five features** (#675, #677 ×2, #757 ×2, #821 ×3). The repair is cheap and the failure is loud, which is exactly why it keeps being tolerated rather than fixed — but it also means `phase` is not trustworthy as a resume signal unless the resuming session re-derives it, which `feature-resume` does.
> - **Scope item (4) — did the step-8 fallow preflight and the gate's phase-write removal ship? NEITHER.** `scripts/verify-gate.sh` still excludes `ci:fallow*` (lines 7, 195) with no step-8 preflight anywhere in the feature skill; and the gate still writes `phase` unconditionally (`feature-state.sh:50` is guarded, but `verify-gate.sh` calls `set-phase gate_green` on success), so running the gate after review completion rewinds `reviewed` → `gate_green`. **That rewind fired twice more during #757's ship** — once on resume, once after the evidence commit — and both times had to be repaired by hand with `set-phase reviewed`. Now four occurrences across three features (#675, #677 ×2, #757 ×2). The fallow half caused no incident here only because the branch happened to be clean.

PR **#700** (#671 reviews list surface) is deliberately **not counted** — docs-only, zero runtime lines, so it adds no architectural entropy. **Possible undercount, flagged not absorbed (2026-08-08):** PR **#712** (#678 Reviews list + On-demand generation, merged `e447ee5b`) carries substantial runtime surface — 71 files, ~9.3k insertions across `src/lib/past-replies/`, a new `/api/dashboard/reviews/[reviewId]/generate` route, `src/lib/costs/tracker.ts` and two publisher files — yet appears in no entry above and has no post-merge sweep commit on `main`. The #695 sweep did **not** add it, to avoid double-counting if that feature's own step 8 is still pending; if it is not, the true figure is **17** and the overdue multiple is 3.4×. Whoever runs the architecture pass should reconcile this first. **Second possible undercount, flagged not absorbed (2026-08-08):** PR **#716** (#680 legacy backfill counters DROPPED, merged `7b5718c1`) appears in no entry above either. What is observable: 16 files, ~1.6k insertions; a schema change — `supabase/migrations/103_drop_legacy_backfill_counters.sql` drops four columns from `zernio_backfill_runs` and has been applied to **both** Supabase projects in lockstep; it is the declared point of no return for the Consented Review Import epic (#667), after which ADR 0015 §4's revert-only backout no longer suffices; and its own record states **zero production TypeScript changes**. Its own post-merge sweep, PR **#718** (`0b3a1d20`), touched `lab-notes.md`, `review-ingestion.md`, `currents.md` and `progresstracking.md` but **not this file**, so the omission looks like a missed step rather than a deliberate exclusion — unlike PR #700, which is recorded above as deliberately not counted. Its true architectural weight is not assessed here: a migration that only drops already-dead columns and a migration that reshapes a live contract are not the same entropy, and deciding which this is belongs to the pass, not to the counter. If #716 counts, the figure is **17** (3.4×); if both #712 and #716 count, **18** (3.6×).

> 2026-08-10 pass — verdicts (CLOSED / FIX / PROTOCOL / DEFER) live in

Added by #647 (#643), for the next pass to weigh — three workflow-mechanism
findings, none blocking, all recorded in `lab-notes.md` under "2026-08-03":

- **`verify-gate.sh` does not own its DB precondition.** An intake could declare
  `pnpm test:db:setup` — which DROPs schema `public` — as a `verification_command`,
  and the gate launches those in parallel with the `test` row. It produced a fully
  spurious RED. Candidate fix: run DB provisioning serially before the fan-out, or
  reject destructive commands from `verification_commands`.
- **`migration-immutability-guard.sh` keys on the migrations-log head, not on
  "applied".** Every in-place amendment of a local-only migration is a false
  positive, and this workflow produces that shape whenever the migrations-log task
  runs before the ship lane. Candidate fix: a per-entry applied marker.
- **`reclassify` counts workflow artifacts against the implementation they
  describe.** #643 fired at 2.94× with production code at 435 lines and 37/37 files
  in-surface; 1,537 of the counted lines were the plan, deviations, intake and
  handoff. Candidate fix: exclude `docs/superpowers/plans/**`, and budget
  production and test volume as two separate estimates.

Also carried, not a code finding: the RISK=high lane's staging ride makes
`git merge --ff-only main` structurally impossible afterwards — `main` gets a
squash commit while `staging` carries a real merge, so reconciling needs a
verified-identical-trees `reset --hard` + `--force-with-lease`. This will recur on
every high-risk feature; feature.md step 8's post-merge instruction should say so.

Added by #654 (#644), for the next pass to weigh. **The staging-reconcile item above
recurred exactly as predicted** — `staging` ended 61 ahead / 1 behind after the squash
merge and needed `reset --hard origin/main` + `--force-with-lease`. Second occurrence, so
it is no longer a hypothesis; `deployment-guide.md` step 5 does document the reset, but
feature.md step 8 still says "fast-forward `staging` onto `main`", which is the instruction
that cannot work. One code finding and two workflow-mechanism findings:

- **`inject`-only suites are structurally blind to the raw request line — and one such
  blind spot was hiding a live limiter bypass for all five ingress providers.** Absolute-form
  request targets (RFC 9112 §3.2.2) resolve normally through `find-my-way` while leaving
  `request.url` absolute, defeating the decoded-path arm of the ingress-scope test; a
  mutation run reported the surviving arm as dead logic because `app.inject()` normalises
  the target and every rate-limit test in the repo goes through it. Closed for #644 with a
  real-socket test. **The pass should ask what else assumes a normalised request line** —
  signature verification, path-prefix auth checks, and the WAF-adjacent rules are the
  candidates, and none of them has a raw-socket test today.
- **`feature-cleanup.sh` silently cleans nothing when run from inside a feature worktree.**
  It derives `MAIN_ROOT` from its own cwd and then skips `[ "$WT" = "$MAIN_ROOT" ]`, so the
  worktree you are standing in — the one that just merged — is the one it cannot remove. It
  reports SKIP/KEEP rows for everything else and exits 0, which reads as success. Candidate
  fix: resolve `MAIN_ROOT` from `git worktree list`'s first entry (the real main checkout)
  rather than `rev-parse --show-toplevel`, or refuse to run from a non-main checkout.
- **feature.md step 8's post-merge sweep is described as impossible from the shipping
  session, and it is not.** #643's lab note concluded the sweep "must run from the main
  checkout AFTER `feature-cleanup.sh` removes the worktree". That route was unavailable here
  (main checkout on an unrelated branch; cleanup skips its own worktree). Parking
  `.feature/intake.json` and branching the shipping worktree off `origin/main` satisfies both
  guard rules — same checkout for containment, no intake for arming — and needs no
  launch-time env var. Candidate fix: document park-and-branch as the sanctioned route, and
  note that the branch must come off `origin/main`, since local `main` lags every squash merge.

Added by #659 (#645), for the next pass to weigh. **The staging-reconcile item is now
on its THIRD consecutive occurrence** (#647, #654, #659) — it is not a hypothesis and
not bad luck, it is what the high-RISK lane structurally produces. #659 additionally
hit the *inverse* shape: `staging` was left holding an OLDER auth tree than `main`
because a second branch rode staging mid-flight and landed on main as a squash, so
"reconcile" meant moving staging **forward**, not discarding work. feature.md step 8
still says "fast-forward `staging` onto `main`". Two workflow findings and one
code-shaped finding:

- **A refactor can blind a static security audit, and the type checker will not
  notice.** Extracting the three admin suppressions routes' shared preamble into a
  `withAal2JsonBody` wrapper typechecked, passed every route test, and turned
  `ci:admin-aal2` RED on all three routes — that audit is a file-level TEXT scan for
  `requireAdminWithAal2`, so three genuinely gated routes read as ungated. The audit
  exposes a `GATED_SHARED_HELPERS` allowlist that would have silenced it; taking it
  would have traded a greppable gate for a shorter file. **The pass should ask which
  other `ci:*` audits are text scans whose premise a legal refactor can void** —
  `ci:static-policy`, `ci:tenant-scope` and `ci:outbox-seam` are all string-matching
  audits over source, and none has a test proving it still fires after its target is
  wrapped, aliased, or re-exported.
- **`fallow` is a CI-only gate and nothing local tells you so.** `verify-gate.sh`
  runs 28 checks and not this one; CLAUDE.md says `ci:fallow` "gates nothing", which
  is true of that script but not of `fallow.yml`'s `ci:fallow:coverage`, which is a
  PR check. #645 was fully green locally through two review rounds and failed fallow
  the first time a PR existed. Candidate fix: either add fallow to `verify-gate.sh`
  or say plainly in CLAUDE.md that a green local gate does not predict the fallow
  check.
- **Local `fallow` and CI `fallow` disagree, and the local one invents findings.**
  Without `--coverage`, CRAP is estimated from export references; locally that
  reported `ContactRow` as HIGH (63.6 CRAP) while the coverage-informed CI run did
  not flag it at all. Anyone refactoring to satisfy local fallow is chasing a number
  CI does not compute. Candidate fix: make `ci:fallow` refuse to run without a
  coverage file, or label estimated findings as non-gating in the output.

Added by #682 (#674), for the next pass to weigh. **The staging-reconcile item is now
on its FOURTH consecutive occurrence** (#647, #654, #659, #682) — `staging` ended 21
ahead / 1 behind and needed the `deployment-guide.md` §5 `reset --hard origin/main` +
`--force-with-lease`. feature.md step 8 *still* says "fast-forward `staging` onto
`main`". Four occurrences is no longer a finding to weigh; it is a one-line doc fix
that keeps being deferred. Three findings, all about **checks that cannot see what
they claim to check**:

- **`describeWithDb` asserts nothing about the schema, and the convention text reads
  as though it does.** CLAUDE.md says live-DB suites "**assert** their schema
  (`assertSchemaReady`) instead of applying migrations", which is a statement about
  the *convention*; `src/__tests__/helpers/describe-with-db.ts` only gates on
  `TEST_DATABASE_POOL_URL`. #674's plan asserted the helper "handles the schema
  assertion" and shipped a suite with no assertion; the nearest precedent
  (`read.integration.test.ts`) has the same gap, so copying the neighbour propagates
  it. 32 suites do call it, so the convention is real but unenforced. **The pass
  should ask whether `ci:live-db-suites` — which already parses every live-DB suite —
  should require an `assertSchemaReady` call**, and whether the requirement should be
  checked against what the transaction under test *writes* rather than what the suite
  reads (#674 shipped a first fix asserting 071 for tables it reads, missing
  `agent_runs.parent_run_id` from 088 that the code under test writes).
- **`ci:live-db-suites` enumerates via `git ls-files` and is blind to untracked
  files, and its pass output cannot evidence otherwise.** A new suite audited before
  staging passes vacuously; and because `scripts/ci/live-db-suites-audit.ts` prints
  filenames only in its failure branch, no passing run can ever show *which* files it
  inspected. This is the #590 "checks that appear to run but exercise nothing" class,
  in a check added to close that class. Candidate fix: print the inspected count on
  success, and fail when a `*.integration.test.ts` in the working tree is untracked.
- **Plan-embedded code is a third-class citizen of every quality gate.** Three plan
  review rounds on #674 accumulated a wrong column name (`run_id` for `agent_run_id`),
  a missing assertion, a dead type cast, and a vacuous audit step — none visible to
  lint, tsc, or any test, because a plan is prose to every tool in the repo. It is
  then executed nearly verbatim by a subagent. **The pass should ask whether plans
  containing fenced `sql`/`typescript` blocks deserve any mechanical check at all** —
  even just extracting identifiers and grepping them against the migrations would have
  caught the one defect that would have stopped the keystone's only live-DB suite from
  running.

Added by #685/#686 (#673), for the next pass to weigh. **The staging-reconcile item
reached its FIFTH occurrence** (#647, #654, #659, #682, #685) — same divergence, same
`reset --hard` + `--force-with-lease`, this time additionally blocked by the harness
permission classifier so the owner ran it by hand. feature.md step 8 still says
"fast-forward". One new finding:

- **A locked intake has no channel for ticket amendments posted after its lock.**
  #670's resolution amended #673's enum to three values ~17 minutes after #673's
  intake was signed; three plan-review rounds and two branch-review rounds later,
  migration 100 shipped the four-valued CHECK to both environments, corrected same-day
  by migration 101 (#686). Every review validates against the intake — by design —
  so an amendment that only lives on the ticket is structurally invisible. **The pass
  should ask where the re-read belongs** (plan sign-off and the step-8 preflight are
  the candidates in the lab-notes entry) and whether the amendment protocol should
  REQUIRE the amending session to open a PR against the running feature's intake
  copies rather than posting a comment and hoping.

Added by #691 (#677), for the next pass to weigh. **The staging-reconcile item
reached its SIXTH occurrence** (#647, #654, #659, #682, #685, #691) — same
divergence, same `reset --hard` + `--force-with-lease`. feature.md step 8 still
says "fast-forward `staging` onto `main`", which **cannot** work after a squash
merge: `main` gets one new commit whose parent is the old `main`, so the branch
tip is not an ancestor and `--ff-only` always fails. This is not an
environment quirk to be re-diagnosed each ship; it is the deterministic
consequence of squash-merging, and the workflow text is simply wrong. **The pass
should decide whether step 8 says "reset `staging` to `main`" outright**, and
whether the tree-equality check (`git rev-parse origin/staging^{tree}` ==
`origin/main^{tree}`) that makes the force-push provably content-neutral belongs
in the workflow as a required precondition. Two further findings:

- **The `fallow`-outside-the-local-gate class reached its THIRD consecutive
  occurrence** (#659, #689, #691), each costing a post-open fix commit, a gate
  re-run, and — on RISK=high branches — a staging re-ride. The #689 lab note
  already prescribes running `pnpm ci:fallow` before opening the PR; prescribing
  it in lab-notes has now failed twice. **The pass should ask whether the
  step-8 preflight should RUN it** rather than document it, given
  `verify-gate.sh` deliberately excludes `ci:fallow*` and that exclusion is
  correct on its own terms. Note the clone half has a surface-budget
  interaction: fallow matches clones repo-wide, so the reported partner can sit
  outside the branch's surface and "fix it there" costs an amendment.
- **`verify-gate.sh` and the /feature workflow both own `.feature/state.json`'s
  `phase`, and the gate's write is unconditional.** Running the gate after
  review completion rewinds `reviewed` → `gate_green`, which makes a resumed
  session re-enter step 7 and open a review round against feature.md's cap on
  already-reviewed code. Hit twice in the #677 ship session. This is the mirror
  of #675's opposite trap (the phase had to be *held* at `executing`). **The
  pass should decide who owns that field** — a gate that only ever advances, a
  gate that writes `lastGreenGateSha` but never `phase`, or an explicit
  `--phase` flag.

Review-forced structure worth naming: raw issue bodies are QUARANTINED to a credential-free read-only classifier (the push-capable controller consumes only schema-validated seeds — prompt-injection resistance is structural at both ends); high-risk issues (predicate verbatim from `/feature` step 0, approval/auto-approval paths included) are PREPARED but never executed, since the pipeline's high-risk intake sign-off cannot be pre-authorized — this deliberately superseded the owner's grill answer and was re-approved at sign-off; trivial issues PROMOTE to the small lane (the trivial lane creates no intake/state and cannot pass the red-team `reviewed` gate); edit-surface reservations PERSIST ACROSS RUNS under a separate GLOBAL lock and release only on attested merge/closure — run-end release would hand the next run a collision against unchanged main; a non-no-op rebase VOIDS red-team evidence and forces a complete re-pass (the checker's staleness rule, honored rather than fought); resume claims automatic recovery ONLY for six enumerated states and parks every dirty tree ("run-owned dirt" has no deterministic test). `queue-state.mjs` uses a LEASE+token run lock, not PID-liveness — the controller is a session whose tool calls are short-lived processes, so PID rules would mark every lock stale instantly; the reserve/refuse path was restructured after a self-caught defect where `process.exit(2)` inside the lock's callback skipped `finally` and leaked the global lock for its 60s staleness window (now pinned by a test). `SKIP_ENV_COPY=1` (paired with the pre-existing `SKIP_INSTALL=1`) keeps staging/provider credentials out of executor worktrees, closing the window where `pnpm install` lifecycle scripts ran with copied `.env*.local` present; the token ceiling is named an ADVISORY spend threshold (refuse-next-step semantics, no false hard-bound claim). 12 new tests; plan + 6-round debate log in `~/.claude/grill-codex/20260828-002542/`. |

That reframing is the whole change: **the wide space was an arrangement problem wearing a padding problem's clothes.** `.content--dash` mirrors the existing `.content--analytics` precedent exactly — `max-width: none`, 32px horizontal padding **matched to the topbar's**, because the topbar is a SIBLING of `.content` and the two can only align by using the same number; the 1140px cap bought nothing at 1440px and pure gutters above it. Zone gap 22→16px, dashboard cards 24/26→20/22px, hero 26/28→22/24px. **Three arrangement changes, each removing chrome rather than content:** (a) the agent-team strip loses its wrapper card — three persona tiles inside a fourth card is a card in a card, and the tiles become Harbor cards on the canvas, the same shape `.approvals` already uses one row below; (b) the two doorway strips (approvals → Reviews, link → Analytics) share one zone via the new `.dash-pair`, which is where two full-width bands carrying one line each were costing the most height, and which drops to one column at **1200px, not the shell's 768** — a threshold it now SHARES with `.recap-body`, arrived at by screenshotting 1280/1200/1199/1024/900 rather than by guessing: at 1024px the sidebar and canvas gutters leave ~696px of content, so a half-width strip is 340px and its subtitle wraps to five lines, and at 900px the recap's fixed 30rem plot column left the sentence ~60px and wrapped the headline one word per line. Both defects were invisible to every test and were caught only by looking at an intermediate width — the §8 lesson applied to a breakpoint rather than to source order; (c) the recap's chart is anchored flush to its card's right padding with bars sized to fill the column exactly (`minmax(0, 1fr) minmax(0, 30rem)`, 55px cap), so the surplus reads as one column gap and the `8 weeks` caption sits under the last bar instead of floating past it. `.section-enter-7` retired with its last consumer. **Measured, same account and same data:** desktop 1440px 1423→1193px tall (−230, −16%) with the usable row 68px wider; mobile 390px 2548→2357px (−191). **What this pass deliberately did NOT fix, and why it is not a spacing bug:** the weekly recap's content — one sentence, two numbers, an eight-bar sparkline — measures ~764px against a ~1046px card. | 2026-08-29 | Migration 116 `refusal_events` — the **Refusal ledger**'s table, slice 1 of 7 (issue #1008, map #994, schema locked in #1001) | `supabase/migrations/116_refusal_events.sql` (new) · `scripts/ci/tenant-tables.ts` · `docs/security/rls-inventory.md` · `src/lib/account-offboarding/{table-disposition,purge-verification}.ts` · `worker/jobs/account-offboarding/purge-stages.ts` · `CONTEXT.md` (4 LOCKED terms) · `docs/adr/0023-refusal-log-owner-safe-projection.md` (new) · `.claude/reference/migrations-log.md` · 4 test files | Schema + worker purge surface. **No write site and no read** — the insert helper is #1009 and there are eleven write sites in total; this change left the split point unsettled on purpose (D16), and **#1009 settled it: the FIRST site, `autonomy_suspended`, lands in #1009 beside the helper, and the other TEN across #1010–#1013** — the **Restraint tab** #1014. **Why a table rather than a read-time projection:** the parent effort derived this surface from seven existing tables and could not ORDER it — two of the seven lanes carry no immutable timestamp of their own — and `account_audit_log` has no location key, so joining it through `businesses` multiplied one account-wide suspension by the location count. `occurred_at` (writer-supplied, `NOT NULL`, the single retention clock) and the biconditional CHECK C3 (`business_id IS NOT NULL` ⇔ not `autonomy_suspended`) exist because of those two defects specifically, not as generic hygiene. **The adversarial plan review found a real defect in the LOCKED schema, and it is why the gate earned its cost.** #1001's C5 ADMITTED the row it exists to forbid: with `refusal_code = 'winner'` against `all_refusal_codes = ARRAY['different', NULL]`, `= ANY(…)` finds no TRUE and one NULL so it returns NULL, the arm collapses to NULL, `NULL OR FALSE` is NULL, and **a CHECK is satisfied by NULL**. Reproduced against a live database with two `TEMP` tables — locked expression vs corrected — before a line of the fix was written; eight probe rows, all eight now correct. Two clauses close it: `array_position(all_refusal_codes, NULL) IS NULL` forbids a NULL member outright, and an outer `IS TRUE` applies C1's wrapper to the same NULL class. C1–C4 were checked for the same class and are clean (C1 already had the wrapper; C2/C3/C4 compare expressions that are each never NULL). **C1's wrapper cannot be proven by inserting a row** — the standalone `refusal_source` CHECK rejects an unmapped lane before C1 evaluates — so its test reads `pg_get_constraintdef`, and it asserts the deparsed `ELSE NULL::boolean` is PRESENT rather than absent: Postgres normalises a `CASE` with no `ELSE` into one, so asserting absence tests pg's deparser instead of the constraint (it failed for exactly that reason on the first run). **A forced-consequence chain nobody declared up front**: `accountLinkedTablesFromMigrations()` parses migration files for `account_id … REFERENCES accounts(id)`, so the table forces a `TABLE_DISPOSITIONS` row; `hard_delete`/`must_be_absent` forces a `verificationQuery` arm and an account-scoped `DELETE` in `runContentPurgeStage`, because **no cascade reaches this table** (its only FKs are to `accounts` and `businesses`, both `retain_deidentified`, neither carrying a referential action); and `TENANT_TABLES` membership forces a `docs/security/rls-inventory.md` row, since `rls-audit.test.ts` asserts `REQUIRED_RLS_TABLES === TENANT_TABLES`. `TENANT_TABLES` is **two structures** — the array and the scope-column map — and editing only the map left the completeness test red. **That chain changed the ship order.** Issue #1008 prescribed merge-then-promote on the premise that no deployed code names the table; the purge `DELETE` broke it. A `42P01` there aborts the purge transaction and `PurgeWorker` TERMINALIZES the offboarding after bounded retries (`markPurgeTerminalFailure` + `markOutboxFailed`) — promoting afterwards does not reopen it. The first correction (migration first in every environment) fixed that by creating a worse hazard: production DDL ahead of the PR, i.e. unreviewed CI-ungated SQL. `deployment-guide.md` §II.5 states the PR-first shape and staging-DDL-before-prod-DDL; the production-DDL-before-MERGE step is this branch's own inference from it plus the `42P01` hazard, not a sentence that section carries. The order that holds both properties: **CI green → staging DDL → staging ride → owner approval → production DDL → merge**. **Rollback is a PR revert, never a down migration** — the same parser reads `CREATE` statements rather than net schema, so a 117 `DROP` would not release the disposition row; the revert removes the migration file and every dependent row together, and an empty table with no reader, writer or registry entry is inert. **Deny-all RLS is load-bearing, not a default**: `all_refusal_codes` is non-owner-facing under ADR 0023 and **RLS filters rows, not columns**, so an owner-readable policy could not withhold it. Vocabulary carried from the parked `feat/refusal-log` branch and corrected in three places — seven lanes not six (#1003), eight categories not seven (#998), and the tab does add a migration; the parked ADR's two stated limits are both closed and say so. Three plan-review rounds (round 3 killed unfinished after 40 min, recorded): round 1 found the C5 hole and four more, round 2 found four defects introduced by round 1's own fixes. Then THREE red-team passes: round 1 found a P0 false doc claim plus a test that could not fail, round 2 found five instances of ONE class — a fact stated in two artifacts and corrected in only one — and a third pass, run fresh after the branch was rebased onto `main`, found a SECOND P0 of the very same shape in a SIBLING test the earlier rounds never reached: `purge-verification.test.ts` mocks `sql` and asserts SQL TEXT, so two semantically meaningful mutants survived — one narrowing the WHERE clause to hide exactly the account-wide rows the case is NAMED for, one renaming the count alias so `countViolations` reads `undefined ?? 0` and reports a CLEAN purge over surviving tenant data. Fixed with the repo's own #643 pattern: a live-DB arm that seeds both shapes and reads the count back out of a real database. Both mutants verified killed by measurement, not by inspection. **The lesson is the recurrence**: fixing a could-not-fail test in one file is not a fix until its siblings are swept, which is D15 applied to tests rather than to prose. 23 promised tests, all present verbatim; migration LOCAL-ONLY at merge. |

| 2026-08-30 | The **Refusal ledger**'s write path — slice 2 of 7 (issue #1009, map #994), and the change #1008 named as the one that would settle its deviation **D16** | `src/lib/refusals/` (`vocabulary.ts`, the insert helper) · `src/lib/auto-approval/suspension.ts` (the first write site) · `worker/background/pollers/ArchivalPoller.ts` (the retention arm) · `scripts/ci/tenant-scope-allowlist.ts` · `.claude/reference/{arch-review-log,migrations-log,lab-notes,env-vars}.md` · `docs/{currents,progresstracking,directory,eservices-variables}.md` · `docs/superpowers/plans/feat-1008-…-deviations.md` | Worker + a write inside the auto-approval suspension transaction, plus the D16 sweep. **D16 IS SETTLED: the FIRST Refusal write site lands here** — `autonomy_suspended`, one site, inside `suspendAccountAutonomy`; the other ten across #1010–#1013. **The evidence is not an artifact count, and that is the point.** #1008 counted the copies, found them 3-to-3, and correctly declined to pick a winner — but a tie is the signal that the deciding evidence sits OUTSIDE the artifacts being counted. A lane must SAY `'transaction_time'`; it cannot arrive there by omission, which is why the helper's `occurredAt` is required rather than optional. **The method is D16's own lesson applied to D16:** the sweep ran BEFORE the edit direction was chosen, and every citation here is a SYMBOL rather than a line number — #1008's D14 proved a line citation into a file the same change edits cannot be correct at both ends of that change. **What six adversarial rounds actually bought, recorded because the cost was real.** **(a) A reversal.** The plan rejected a per-lane discriminated union on the ground that a TypeScript copy would be "a second authority" over migration 116's CHECKs. Branch review found that conflates STORAGE authority with API shape — 116 must stay the storage authority because TypeScript cannot protect SQL written by another process, but that never required the API to accept states already known illegal. Two axes reached it independently, one at `high`. `WriteRefusalEventInput` is now a mapped type over `RefusalSource`, so an eighth lane without a shape is a compile error rather than a runtime `23514` aborting a deciding transaction; `refusalCategory` is deliberately NOT narrowed per lane, because #1001 declined to pin a lane's category. **(b) Three plan rounds designed on a library behaviour none of them read.** Rounds 1, 2 and 3 each found a different bug in one five-line kill-switch warning, and every fix rested on `Sentry.captureMessage` throwing on delivery failure. It does not: `@sentry/node` 10.55.0 returns an event id immediately (even with no client), buffers the event, sends asynchronously and **catches transport failures internally**. So "capture succeeded" only ever meant "capture was queued", and a real outage latched the flag with the warning undelivered — the exact permanent silence rounds 2 and 3 were chasing, by a route none examined. The shipped design makes each transport promise only what it can keep: the structured log line is primary and durable, emitted first and outside the Sentry guard; the rate limit is per DISABLED EPISODE, not per process; no `flush()`, because awaiting delivery would put network latency on the deciding transaction's path. **The plan rounds could not have caught this — a plan does not name a version.** **(c) Nine instances of ONE class: a fact corrected in one artifact and left stale in another.** The D16 sweep table was short four separate times, including twice inside its own sweep and once against a copy this branch had just written; a commit of mine claiming to be a sweep contradicted a file it had edited in the same commit, and my fix for THAT was short by four more. The rule that works is mechanical rather than attentive: **grep the FACT across every artifact, then RE-GREP after fixing** — the re-grep caught four of the nine. **(d) Three false claims about one test, each written while fixing the previous one.** Red-team round 1 found a comment claiming coverage the test lacked; the fix asserted something else equally false; round 2 planted that mutant and it SURVIVED. Underneath sat a real gap — nothing discriminated a helper opening its own connection on the success path, and `ci:db-imports` matches only the `@/lib/db/server` specifier, so a direct `postgres(…)` in `src/lib/refusals/` was caught by nothing. Test 29 closes it and **kills the mutant by measurement** (30 s timeout vs a 5 ms baseline), with the mechanism reproduced in isolation as `55P03` — the private connection's insert needs `FOR KEY SHARE` on the `accounts` row `lockAutonomyAccount` holds. **What broke the chain was not care; it was planting the mutant.** A claim about what a test catches is checkable in a minute, and none of three authors checked it. **Red-team totals:** 10 mutants planted, 9 killed, 1 arbiter-reclassified equivalent then killed by a follow-up; no P0 across three passes. Independently verified TRUE: the flag-OFF byte-identical path, "no migration 117", `NOW()` = `transaction_timestamp()` (also measured live), all seven CI audits, and a PII/secret sweep. **OVERRIDE, recorded rather than buried:** `red-team-status.mjs` refuses this branch on exactly one ground — `round must be 1 or 2` (`:253`) — because the owner-authorized RT-8 fix voided round 2's own evidence and the attestation round that re-established it is over cap. Every other check passes (forcing `round:2` in memory yields zero shape errors and a recomputed `CLEAR`). The attesting agent **declined to lower the number**, correctly: falsifying a round count is the defect class this run caught nine times. Owner took the `reviewed` transition with the objection documented (deviations D26). **The tooling gap is real and is NOT this branch's**: an owner-authorized late fix is currently unshippable, and patching the checker from inside a feature branch was rejected — it would spend a third surface amendment, trigger the abort criteria, and set the precedent of a branch loosening its own gate. Filed separately. 29 promised tests, all present and active; 26 deviations, all ACCEPT; gate green at 31/31 with three live-DB suites genuinely executing. |

Two small fixes landed: `bash-write-guard.sh` sets `-f` (its unquoted token loop globbed `*` against the cwd) and exits before the session lookup and both `git worktree list` calls when the command carries no write-shaped substring; `typecheck-on-stop.sh` lints with `--cache` (1.7 s cold to 0.7 s). | 2026-09-02 | The admin-onboarding compensating rollback could never delete a row — found by Phase H's first production attempt, not by a test | `src/lib/onboarding/admin-onboarding-service.ts` · `src/lib/onboarding/__tests__/admin-onboarding-service.test.ts` · `src/lib/onboarding/__tests__/admin-onboarding-rollback.integration.test.ts` (new) · `docs/runbooks/evidence/2026-09-01-phase-h-beta-launch-evidence.md` · `.claude/reference/lab-notes.md` | **One production file: the delete order in `rollbackProvisionedBusiness`.** It ran `job_outbox → users → accounts → businesses`, but `fk_businesses_account` is `FOREIGN KEY (account_id) REFERENCES accounts(id)` with **no `ON DELETE CASCADE`** — so deleting `accounts` while the `businesses` row still points at it raises `23503`, the enclosing `sql.begin` aborts, and every earlier delete rolls back with it. **Not a partial cleanup; a total one, swallowed by the caller's `catch`.** The function could never succeed: it is reached only when a business row exists, and a business row is exactly what makes it fail. Fixed by moving `businesses` before `accounts`; the operator message no longer claims "partially failed" and now names the required order; the doc comment justified the old order via the `businesses → channel_enrollments` cascade, which is real but is not the edge that blocks. **The mocked characterization suite pinned the broken order as CORRECT for as long as the bug lived** — a mock `sql` has no foreign keys, so a test over emitted SQL text proves which statements were issued, never whether they succeed, and the one precondition guaranteeing failure is the one a mock cannot represent. Added a live-DB sibling asserting the ROWS ARE GONE, plus a **negative control** that replays the pre-fix order and asserts it still raises `23503` and leaves all three rows, so a future cascade-adding migration turns the control red instead of letting the suite pass vacuously (5 + 3 tests pass, the latter against real Postgres). **A correction to the fix, found by executing it:** a third edge, `accounts.owner_user_id → users.id`, is `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`, as is `users.account_id` — deferred means checked at COMMIT, so the order is correct only inside one explicit transaction; run standalone, the first users delete raises `accounts_owner_user_id_fkey`. **How it surfaced is the durable part.** Phase H's H.0b failed on an SMTP `535 "Authentication credentials invalid"`: the 2026-09-01 Resend rotation replaced `RESEND_API_KEY` and deleted the old key, while Supabase Auth's SMTP password — a *fifth* holder of that credential, in a different provider's dashboard — still held the dead one, taking invites, password resets and email confirmations down for ~7 hours undetected. The rotation had verified every consumer it knew about with fingerprints, a boot gate and a signed webhook; `ROT-D7`'s "capture the provider's key list" finds which keys exist, never who uses them. Both lessons promoted to lab-notes. Three orphan rows were cleaned from production in one transaction, owner-approved, verified by absence. |

| 2026-09-02 | #1079 — `REFUSAL_LEDGER_ENABLED` could not stop a Refusal-ledger throw aborting the response persist, the one transaction the flag exists to protect | `src/lib/refusals/write.ts` · `worker/jobs/response-generation/persist.ts` · `worker/jobs/response-generation/__tests__/{persist,refusal-write.integration}.test.ts` · `src/lib/refusals/__tests__/write.test.ts` | **Two production files, 26 executable lines; no new module, no new dependency, no schema.** The flag was read INSIDE `writeRefusalEvent`, but `recordPolicyGateRefusal` resolved a Refusal category three lines earlier — so an unmapped or unranked block reason threw with the switch already off, and the throw escaped `withLedgerAttribution` and reported as a generic persist failure. Four things a later pass should not re-derive. (a) **The flag read is SIDE-EFFECT-FREE, and that is a contract — the word is not "pure".** `isRefusalLedgerEnabled()` reads the flag and returns: no logging, no Sentry, no latch mutation. It is deliberately NOT called pure, because `vocabulary.ts` defines that word for this same feature area as "no DB handle, no clock, **no flag read**", and this function's whole body is a flag read that must differ between calls; branch review caught the two meanings colliding inside one area. `warnLedgerDisabled`/`endDisabledEpisode` stay private, so a caller cannot claim a ledger gap as a side effect of merely asking whether the ledger is on — a caller that HAS established a gap says so explicitly through the new `noteRefusalLedgerDark`. The first plan draft folded the transports into the predicate; that made a veto-set decision — which records nothing either way — claim the once-per-episode warning, breaking the rule stated in `writeRefusalEvents`' contract (cited BY SYMBOL: the line range this row first carried went stale inside its own commit, because the same 36-line insert that created the predicate moved the rule). (b) **Containment is by NON-EXECUTION**, so with the flag off nothing in the ledger path runs inside the transaction; the resolve moved inside `withLedgerAttribution` so a vocabulary fault reports as `subsystem=refusal_ledger`. (c) **The observability cost was NOT accepted in the end — review proved it avoidable, and the branch closed it.** The plan had conceded that this lane loses the ledger-dark warning with the switch off, on the premise that a guard cannot know whether a row would have been written without running the throwing resolver. That premise is false: map membership is decidable with `Object.hasOwn`, which cannot throw. Guard 4's off-arm now calls `wouldRecordRefusal` and raises the same once-per-episode line through `noteRefusalLedgerDark`. **The predicate is `every`, never `some`** — one `null`-mapped member vetoes the whole row (#1003), so a `some` test would warn about a decision that was never going to be recorded, re-breaking the exact rule the arm exists to respect. The review that found the gap proposed `some`; the shipped form is the correction. One blind spot survives by construction and is recorded in `beta-launch-alerting.md`: an UNMAPPED reason (the #1080 fault class) raises nothing while the switch is off, because it would have thrown rather than written. (d) **`src/app/api/dashboard/refusals/route.ts:76` is a sanctioned SECOND reader** on the READ path and keeps its own literal flag spelling so `ci:env-registry` can see it — the invariant is "single WRITE-PATH owner", never "single reader". Two live reads exist per write and **the window between them is ZERO, not "microseconds"** — no `await` separates them, so a mid-process flip cannot land between them and there is nothing to defend against. The first version of this row claimed a microsecond window; red team and the async axis both measured it as none. (e) **WHICH GUARDS ARE SAFE TO DELETE — RE-MEASURED, AND THE OLD ANSWER WAS WRONG FOR TWO OF THREE.** The guard docblock had carried "deleting guard 1, 2 or 3 individually changes no outcome and kills no test" since branch review round 1, and this branch renumbered it and re-published it as current. Red team re-ran the experiment one guard at a time against the CURRENT tests: **guard 1 deletion KILLS a test** — this branch's own new `held_draft` pin, which did not exist when round 1 measured, so the commit re-asserting the claim is the commit that falsified it; **guard 2 survives 82/82**, the only case where the old claim holds; **guard 3 deletion kills a pre-existing test AND writes a spurious `policy_gate_held` row for a structural non-candidate**, a ledger data-correctness defect that was already false at the fork point and had simply never been measured. The docblock now carries the per-guard numbers instead of the summary sentence. "Defence in depth" is not "free to remove". The promised property is a BICONDITIONAL over the flag (off ⇒ zero resolver calls; on ⇒ exactly one, receiving the generated array verbatim) — the first draft asserted only the off arm, which the guard cannot branch on, making it a tautology with no mutation-killing power. Counter: the pass decides. |
| 2026-09-02 | #1080 — `AUTO_APPROVAL_BLOCK_PRECEDENCE` proved MEMBERSHIP where TOTALITY was needed, so a mapped-but-unranked block reason type-checked and threw inside the response-persist transaction | `src/lib/refusals/vocabulary.ts` · `src/lib/refusals/__tests__/vocabulary.test.ts` · `worker/jobs/response-generation/__tests__/policy-gate-precedence.property.test.ts` | **One production file, 11 executable lines across 4 declarations (86 comment lines, 4 blank — measured AT HEAD), ZERO runtime lines changed** — the transpiled JS is byte-identical to the fork point. **THE "AT HEAD" QUALIFIER IS THE POINT.** This row first said 79, a figure measured at the round-1 tree; the very commit that wrote the row added seven more comment lines and took it to 86, so the number was false the moment it landed (red-team round 2, RT1080-07). Re-measured by classifying every ADDED line of `vocabulary.ts` against the fork point by its first non-whitespace token: 101 added = 86 comment + 11 code + 4 blank. A later reader should re-derive it rather than requote it, or quote the RATIO instead — roughly eight lines of recorded reasoning per line of type — which is the claim that does not go stale. The same 79 still stands, uncorrected, in `.feature/deviations.md` § D5 and in the round-1 review staging notes, which are outside this fix's edit surface. **EVERY CITATION IN THIS ROW IS BY SYMBOL, NEVER BY LINE.** The first version cited `vocabulary.test.ts:513` and `:527`, which this same commit shifted by +5; `:513` then landed on `'below_our_bar',` inside `LOCKED_TIER_ORDER`, plausible enough that a reader misses the miss. `as const satisfies readonly AutoApprovalBlockReason[]` asserts every LISTED member is valid; it says nothing about the converse. The guarantee now sits in the compiler: `RankableBlockReason` (mapped members, keyed on `extends null`) → `UnrankedIn<P>` (parameterized over the ranks) → a private self-consuming `_PrecedenceIsTotal` that fires at its own declaration site. Seven things a later pass should not re-derive. (a) **THE TICKET WAS WRONG about #1003** and building to its wording would have forbidden this branch's own property test. #1003 rejected TWO tests, both about `REFUSAL_CATEGORIES` ORDER (`vocabulary.ts`'s `AUTO_APPROVAL_BLOCK_PRECEDENCE` docblock; `feat-1013-refusal-policy-gate-held-plan.md` § "Two tests that must NOT be written"), and it REQUIRED the veto-vs-precedence exhaustiveness test that ships as `vocabulary.test.ts`'s `'every AutoApprovalBlockReason member appears exactly once across the veto set and the precedence list'`. Deriving COMPLETENESS is allowed; deriving ORDER is not. A third source for this reading — `docs/reviews/refusal-ledger-audit/spec-verification.md` — was DROPPED rather than re-anchored: it is not tracked at `HEAD` and exists only in one working tree, so it cannot carry a load-bearing claim. (b) **Key on `extends null`, never on `RefusalCategory`** — an `Extract<…, RefusalCategory>` spelling makes the assertion depend on the category SET, so a future category rename fires here. (c) **Six spellings compile and prove NOTHING**, including `const _x: Unranked[] = []` and `const _x = [] satisfies Unranked[]`, which match this module's own idiom and would survive review; a seventh, `type Bad<T> = T extends never ? …`, is a FALSE POSITIVE that fails on the HEALTHY tree because a naked type parameter distributes over `never` — the path from a correct instinct to a vacuous ship is seeing that red and "relaxing" it. The tuple wrapper `[T] extends [never]` suppresses distribution. The red step is therefore "the error names the member", never "typecheck fails": both seeds produced `TS2344` naming `autopilot_tier_required` and `daily_cap_exhausted`. (d) **The `unranked` throw is STRUCTURALLY REQUIRED, not defensive** — `Array.prototype.find` returns `T | undefined` unconditionally and the compiler cannot narrow it, so deleting the branch breaks `refusalCode: winner`. This matters because the sibling `veto_member_won_precedence` throw was DELETED ten lines below citing the same working-rules clause; the realistic regression here is not deletion but `find(...)!`, which is the hidden-bug shape that rule bans. (e) **A member listed TWICE in precedence is still uncaught** — `Prec[number]` collapses duplicates — and stays covered at run time by the `new Set(precedence).size` assertion inside `vocabulary.test.ts`'s partition case. Side effect, left in place: the pre-existing `fallow-ignore-next-line unused-export` above `AUTO_APPROVAL_BLOCK_PRECEDENCE` is now STALE, because the test's new `typeof` reference is the first statically visible cross-module consumer where the old `Record<string, unknown>` cast was not. It gates nothing (`ci:fallow --fail-on-issues` exits 0) and it contradicts `.fallowrc.jsonc`'s stated belief that tests are not consumers, so removing it would trade a warn for a real finding later. Counter: the pass decides. (f) **`satisfies` preserves the literals, NOT `as const` — measured, and two comments on this branch originally credited the wrong clause.** Dropping `as const` from either `POLICY_GATE_HELD_REFUSAL_CATEGORIES` or `AUTO_APPROVAL_BLOCK_PRECEDENCE` produces ZERO diagnostics: the `satisfies` clause contextually types the members and the literals survive. What actually breaks the derivation is respelling either as a type ANNOTATION (`const X: Record<…> = {…}` / `const X: readonly AutoApprovalBlockReason[] = […]`) — the map's annotation form makes `_PrecedenceIsTotal` fire on a HEALTHY tree with all 22 members, and the array's annotation form makes the test's anti-vacuity `@ts-expect-error` unnecessary and so `TS2578`. Anyone re-deriving "the `as const` is what protects this" will get it backwards. (g) **A finite witness set is swept, never sampled.** #1080's totality claim has one witness per mapped member — the singleton — so `policy-gate-precedence.property.test.ts` proves it with an exhaustive sweep (`'every mapped non-veto block reason alone resolves to itself, exhaustively'`, caught a seeded hole 30/30 in a mirrored resolver) and keeps the `fast-check` property only for the ORDER dimension a sweep cannot reach. The earlier spelling defended the property with a per-run witness rate; three independent measurements of that rate disagreed and one 30-run trial came back green against a real seeded hole, which is the argument for replacing the estimate rather than refining it. (h) **A SOURCE-TEXT ORACLE MATCHES THE TEMPLATE TEXT, NEVER THE INTERPOLATION.** The two `toContain` assertions that prove `vocabulary.ts` still declares two differently-named faults first included the interpolated expressions (`${code}`, `${blockReasons.join(',')}`); renaming the resolver's loop variable — behaviour-preserving, and moving no fault name — failed the case reporting "the unmapped fault lost its own name", which was untrue. An oracle that reports a false cause on a refactor trains the next reader to distrust it. They now match `` new Error(`unmapped_auto_approval_block_reason: `` and its `unranked` twin — the literal prefix a rename cannot move, and the string support actually greps for — and their failure messages say what was checked (red-team round 2, RT1080-08; the only defect of that round that was in the tests rather than in the prose). **PRECEDENT, named because `working-rules.md` requires it:** the repo's one other compile-time assertion is `restraint-policy.test.ts`'s `'is sentiment-blind at the TYPE level, not only in serialized output'` (`type AssertSentimentBlind<T> = … extends never ? true : never`, consumed by `const … = true`). It stays; it was not copied because its failure is a `TS2322` that never NAMES the offending member, and naming the unranked member is this ticket's whole deliverable — only a constraint violation on `AssertNever<T extends never>` prints it. The exact text, MEASURED under this repo's own `tsc` rather than recalled, is `Type 'true' is not assignable to type 'never'.`; `vocabulary.test.ts`'s precedent docblock quoted it as `'boolean'` until round 2 (RT1080-10), which is the third instance on this branch of the class "the branch states something about itself that is false" — the reason every claim in this row is either measured or attributed. |
| 2026-09-03 | #1082 — `settleGenerationExhaustion` wrote one `refusal_events` row per hole, inside the transaction holding the per-Business advisory lock and the batch row lock, while the set arm built for exactly that shape sat unimported | `src/lib/solicitation/ask-generation.ts` · `src/lib/solicitation/__tests__/{ask-generation.test.ts,ask-generation.integration.test.ts}` | **One production file, 27 executable lines added; no new module, no new dependency, no schema, and no new SQL statement anywhere. THE COMMENT COUNT IS DELIBERATELY NOT QUOTED HERE — RE-DERIVE IT.** This row first said "40 comment, 3 blank — measured AT HEAD", and the very next commit (the review-fix commit, which is prose) took it to 66 and falsified the row inside its own branch. That is the identical failure the #1080 row one entry above records by name, repeated one entry later. The durable claim is the RATIO — roughly two to three lines of recorded reasoning per line of code — and the METHOD: `git diff <fork>..HEAD -- src/lib/solicitation/ask-generation.ts`, classify each added line by its first non-whitespace token. The code count (27) is the half that does not move under a prose fix, which is why it is the half quoted. `writeRefusalEvents` already shipped with #1009 and `commitAskDecisions` already called it; this branch is the second call site. Six things a later pass should not re-derive, and the FIRST is the one this branch got wrong and had to correct under review. (f) **THE BOUND IS `BATCH_CAP` (25), NOT `MAX_DATA_ROWS` (5,000) — THE TICKET, THE INTAKE, THE PLAN AND TWO COMMENTS ALL SHIPPED IT 200-FOLD TOO LARGE.** `holes` filters `ask.status = 'send'`; `commitAskDecisions` writes `status = decision.decision`, `evaluateBatch` (`restraint-policy.ts`) keeps at most `BATCH_CAP` send decisions per batch, and the sole `UPDATE solicitation_asks` touches `agent_run_id` and never `status` — so at most 25 asks in a batch can ever BE a hole and the saving is at most 24 round trips. The 5,000 ceiling is REAL and belongs to the `ask_withheld` lane, where withheld candidates are uncapped; `writeRefusalEvents`' own docblock cites it correctly THERE, and citing a neighbouring lane's constant is how it crossed over. Two review axes disagreed about this in the same wave — the standards axis derived 25 from the cap, the async axis re-derived 5,000 from the candidate window and explicitly wrote "no correction needed" — and the tiebreaker was neither axis but the four-step chain above. **A ceiling quoted from a sibling lane is not evidence about this one.** (g) **CITE BY SYMBOL, ALWAYS — the QUALIFIED version of that rule is a trap, and this branch fell into it twice.** Round 2 falsified the first form ("citations are by symbol"): two bare line citations had survived it and one, `:1632`, did not resolve. The test: *is this document asserting what is true NOW, or recording what was measured THEN?* (h) **A FIGURE THAT MOVES UNDER PROSE EDITS NEEDS ONE OWNER, AND EVERY OTHER MENTION POINTS AT IT.** The comment-line count moved three times on this branch — 40 → 66 → 68 — because each prose fix, including the fixes for this class of error, changed it. This row was corrected to stop quoting it; its twin in `<branch>-deviations.md` § D1 was MISSED in that same sweep and had to be found by a later red-team run. Correcting one mention of a moving figure and leaving its twin is the predictable failure, so the repair is not "fix both" but "let one own it". The reason to batch survives the correction unchanged: a per-row write inside a loop is the shape the set arm exists to prevent, and that argument never depended on the number. **THEN THE STAGING DRILL MEASURED IT AND THE "SMALL" READING WAS ALSO WRONG — 24 ROUND TRIPS IS ~1.6 SECONDS OF LOCK-HELD TIME.** On the staging database, 25 single-row inserts take 1754 ms against 141 ms for one set insert of the same rows (median of three, both rolled back); a round trip costs ~70 ms through PgBouncer to Supabase and ~0.4 ms to a local Docker Postgres. **The integration suite's own timings understate the production saving by two orders of magnitude**, which is why the review — reasoning from the row count and from local runs — nearly concluded the ticket was marginal. Both halves of that reasoning were defensible and the conclusion was still wrong. A ROW COUNT IS NOT A COST: the count correction (25, not 5,000) was right, and the inference "therefore small" needed a measurement it never had. Evidence: `docs/runbooks/evidence/2026-09-03-1082-exhaustion-refusal-batch-drill.md`. (a) **ONLY ONE OF THE LANE'S FOUR WRITERS WAS A LOOP.** The ticket said "worth checking the other seven write sites"; the answer is no. `persistChildRender` (W1/W2) and `regenerateAsk` (W3) each settle ONE render per transaction, so they carry no per-iteration round trip and stay on the single-row arm. The audit that found this looked at call SHAPE, which is why it could not tell them apart. (b) **THE FIX WAS NOT "MOVE THE CALL"; IT WAS "GIVE THE MAPPING ONE READER".** Two arms spelling `refusalSource`, the code axis, the subject axis and the never-the-sentinel clock rule by hand is how they drift, so `outreachWithheldRefusalInput` is extracted and both arms project through it. It is PURE in `vocabulary.ts`'s sense for this area — no `tx`, no clock read, no flag read — which is what makes it safe inside the loop. `recordOutreachWithheldRefusal` keeps the docblock; the builder is its projection, never a second contract. **No private `recordOutreachWithheldRefusals` set wrapper**: it would have one caller. (c) **THE CALL IS UNCONDITIONAL AND THAT IS THE POINT.** `writeRefusalEvents` documents that an empty input issues no statement and returns BEFORE the kill-switch read; a caller-side `if (refusals.length)` would be a second authority for a guarantee `src/lib/refusals/write.ts` already owns — the exact defect class #1001 cites. `commitAskDecisions` (`batch-builder.ts`) calls unconditionally with a possibly-empty `flatMap`, and this follows it. (d) **THE REAL RISK WAS IN THE TEST FILE, NOT THE LANE.** The unit suite mocked only `writeRefusalEvent`. Had the mock stayed one-armed, `refusalCalls()` would have gone blind to the batching lane and FOUR `not.toHaveBeenCalled()` assertions would have named one half of a two-half writer — all still green. `refusalCalls()` now folds both arms and sorts on `invocationCallOrder`. **THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS BULLET CALLED THAT SORT "LOAD-BEARING" AND SAID ARM-ORDER CONCATENATION "MIS-PAIRS EVERY ROW THE MOMENT A SECOND ARM EXISTS". That is happenstance of test order, not a property of the helper — the same "the branch states something false about itself" class the #1080 row above records three times, arrived at independently by the standards axis reading and by red team mutating. The sort is KEPT, for order-INDEPENDENCE rather than for today: the matrix test pairs `refusalCalls()[i]` with the outreach inserts POSITIONALLY, so a later test that interleaves the arms would mis-pair silently, and a global monotonic counter removes that trap for free. **THEN THE FIX FOR THAT P0 ASSERTED A SECOND UNMEASURED "LOAD-BEARING" IN THE SAME BULLET** — it claimed the paired `clearRefusalCalls()` was the thing that really did carry weight. Red team round 2 falsified that too (R2-M1): reverting it to a one-armed `mockClear` leaves 51/51 green, because its only consuming case drives W1 in every phase and W1 is a single-row writer, so the set-arm mock is empty at both clear points. Re-measured here by the same mutation before this sentence was written. **BOTH ARE INERT TODAY AND BOTH ARE KEPT**, as trap-removers rather than as fixes: each costs nothing and each closes a silent mis-pairing that appears the moment a test interleaves the arms or clears a phase that drove a set-arm writer. The transferable lesson is not about either helper — **it is that this branch reached for the word "load-bearing" twice without running the mutation that would have settled it, and was wrong both times.** RUN THE MUTATION BEFORE WRITING THE ADJECTIVE. (e) **AN ABORT TEST, NOT A COLUMN COMPARISON.** Moving the write out of the loop creates one new hazard: the single statement drifting out of `sql.begin` on a later edit, where it would commit on its own connection and record Refusals for fills that never landed. Equal counts cannot see that. The live-DB case uses `abortingExecutor` with a `SELECT 1 / 0` poison (22012) and asserts BOTH surfaces empty. **`insertOutreachRender` deliberately stays per hole** — batching it would make a second writer of `solicitation_outreach` against that module's own docblock — so the win is 2N → N+1 round trips, not 2. Owner confirmed the scope; the residual is a follow-up. Counter: the pass decides. |
