| 2026-09-03 | feat/stripe-customer-redaction — a `delete_data` purge now REDACTS the account's Stripe Customer before the tombstone destroys the last copy of its id, discloses what survives at the processor, and stops a no-account Stripe subscription event from throwing for three days | `src/lib/account-offboarding/stripe-customer-redaction.ts` (new) · `src/lib/account-offboarding/table-disposition.ts` · `src/lib/accounts/billing-locks.ts` · `worker/jobs/account-offboarding/{purge-stages,PurgeWorker}.ts` · `worker/ingress/stripe/{shared,subscription-updated,subscription-deleted,apply-subscription-state}.ts` · `worker/ingress/{shared,stripe}.ts` · `worker/background/pollers/ArchivalPoller.ts` · `src/lib/{legal/content,notifications/email/templates/account-offboarding}.ts` · `scripts/ci/{tenant-scope-allowlist,static-policy-audit}.ts` · `CONTEXT.md` · `docs/directory.md` | **No migration, no new dependency, no schema change; one new module, one new advisory lock, one new `inbound_events.status` value, tenant-scope baseline 373 → 375.** Six things a later pass should not re-derive. (a) **The Customer is REDACTED, never DELETED** — `customers.del` detaches every invoice irreversibly and would destroy the financial record tax retention requires, so it is referenced nowhere and a test asserts the injected client's `del` is never called. The retrieve before the update is NOT optional: `customers.update` MERGES metadata, so a call sending only the offboarding key leaves every prior key live at Stripe. (b) **The stage runs LATE, immediately before the tombstone, and that is a deliberate tradeoff.** Running first would fail fast with nothing destroyed, but a later local failure would then leave a LIVE account whose Stripe billing contact is already wiped — the same hazard the shared-customer guard exists to prevent. Retry stays safe because `account_offboardings.stripe_customer_id` survives until finalize and `loadPurgeOffboarding` re-reads it. (c) **`acquireStripeCustomerBindingLock` is the first CUSTOMER-keyed lock in `billing-locks.ts`**, every other one being account-keyed, because `accounts.stripe_customer_id` carries no unique constraint and one customer may map to several accounts. It lives in `applyStripeSubscriptionStateInternal`, NOT the `UnderEntitlementLock` wrapper: both exported entry points funnel through the internal function, so locking in the wrapper would leave `applyStripeSubscriptionState` outside it and make the declared invariant false. Order is entitlement first, binding second; the redaction stage takes ONLY the binding lock so it cannot join the account → business → entitlement → account cycle. **Its blast radius was seven test suites wider than the plan predicted** — one extra statement on the path of every subscription webhook, and seven sibling ingress suites pin exact mock-SQL statement counts. That cost surface amendment 4 and is recorded as deviation D4; the lesson is that a lock placed on a shared internal path is a change to every caller's statement sequence, not to one file. (d) **`stale_ignored` is the load-bearing detail of the webhook bound, and `processed` was the trap.** `claimInboundEvent` reclaims only `failed` or a stale `received` and returns `duplicate` for `processed`, so acking an age-stale event as `processed` would have silently deduplicated an operator's manual resend from the Stripe dashboard — strictly WORSE than the three days of retries it replaces. The new status is reclaimable, is excluded from `checkFailedInboundEvents`' failed-only alert query (pinned by a test rather than assumed), and `ArchivalPoller` sweeps it on the same 30-day clock so the retained rows do not leak. There is no CHECK constraint on `inbound_events.status`, so no migration; `idx_ie_status` is partial on `status != 'processed'`, so the value is indexed. (e) **The D8 lock excludes simultaneity but NOT the sequence** "redaction wins, count is zero, a writer then binds the redacted Customer to a live account". That residual is ACCEPTED, not closed, because `buildCheckoutSessionParams` passes `customer_email` and never `customer`, so Stripe mints a fresh Customer per Session and no path can bind an existing one to a second account. A test pins that premise, so a future change that starts reusing Customers fails loudly rather than silently re-opening the race. Closing it properly needs a durable retired-customer marker and a migration. (f) **`PROVIDER_DATA_RETENTION` is deliberately SEPARATE from `TABLE_DISPOSITIONS`**, and the separation is load-bearing rather than tidy: `verificationQuery` throws `missing_purge_verification_sql` for any inventory entry with no SQL branch, and a provider object has no SQL, so an entry in the table inventory would fail every purge. Counter: the pass decides. |
| 2026-09-04 | Import offer banner — the two actions get a slot instead of an inline flex (#1087, build #1096) | `console.css` (`.banner-actions`, `.banner-cta--ghost`, `.banner--top` + `.banner-copy`) · `ImportOfferBanner` (`OfferPrompt` markup, `ImportStatusBanner`/`FailedBanner` shell) · `ImportOfferBanner.test.tsx` (+5) | **UI only — no worker, `src/lib`, migration or billing surface.** The plan named the defect from the CSS without rendering it — "the actions float mid-height **and drift left**" — and measurement killed that half twice over: the offer's action edge already matched `ConnectGoogleBanner`'s CTA to the pixel at **both** 390px and 1440px. `margin-left: auto` was never the missing piece. **Three other things were, and two are the same mistake in different clothes: a flex item's default `min-width: auto` is its min-CONTENT width.** No `flex-shrink: 0` on the group → the copy squeezes it, so at 1440px both labels wrapped and at 390px nothing wrapped at all (copy crushed to 95px, banner **507px**; at 360px **585px**). `align-items: center` floated the actions against a copy block four times their height. And two `margin-left: auto` CTAs in one group split the free space BETWEEN themselves — the plan's own fix caused that, and it was measured out rather than argued about. **THE TWO LESSONS ARE BOTH ABOUT EVIDENCE, AND BOTH COST A REVIEW ROUND.** (a) *Grep the selector, not the names you added.* The first draft set `white-space: nowrap` on `.banner-cta` itself and asserted in two places that no other banner could be reached — over an enumeration one call site short. `AutoApprovalClient`'s "Turn off auto-posting" banner went 133px → **212px** tall at 390px and **293px** at 360px. The rule written to stop a squeeze caused one elsewhere. (b) *Two widths either side of a breakpoint prove nothing about the breakpoint.* The first draft gated the phone wrap at `max-width: 640px`, but the console's reading column is **not monotonic in viewport width** — the sidebar hides below 769px, so the column is widest at 768px and **narrowest one pixel later at 769px** (517px) when the 240px rail returns. At 769px that produced **exactly the pathology it was written to remove**: a 105px ribbon in a 447px banner. Same trap as the accepted #1086 finding, one breakpoint over. The fix is to delete the breakpoint: a percentage `flex-basis` (`flex: 1 1 50%`) resolves against the **container**, so the actions wrap whenever the banner's inner width falls under ~620px, wherever that happens. Swept seven pixel floors and three percentages; a pixel floor has to satisfy two opposing constraints at once and the window between them, if any, is a few pixels wide — round 1 and round 2 measured that window differently because their harnesses disagreed on the action group's width by 7px, which is itself the argument: a mechanism turning on a 4px window that moves with font metrics is the wrong mechanism whichever harness is right. `50%` needs no window. **Evidence for a UI ticket is a width SWEEP, not a pair of screenshots** (14 widths, `.content--dash`, real brand faces, `getBoundingClientRect` beside every image) — a pair is what let both defects through. Review also found the `failed` state losing its terminal screen-reader announcement (it early-returned a different component type at the shell's position, remounting the live region with the text already in place); one shell now serves all four post-offer states. Every new test mutation-checked — including one mutation that PASSED and was itself the bug: keeping `BannerShell` at the position reconciles fine, so only a component-type change breaks it. **A verification round then caught three more, and the sharpest is a general one: `:not()` does not disable a declaration, it un-shadows whatever was underneath.** A `:hover:not(:disabled)` written to stop a disabled ghost button lighting up instead exposed the PRIMARY hover rule beneath it, painting that button solid accent-700 under muted text at **1.45:1 contrast** — worse than the defect it fixed. An override must outrank what it overrides (0-0-4-0 against 0-0-3-0), not merely be absent. The round also found a rule of mine that did nothing under a comment saying it did (`flex-wrap` on a `flex: 0 0 auto` group can never engage; `max-width: 100%` is what gives it something to do), and a test whose node-identity assertion a nested second shell walks straight past. **And it could not reproduce several of the numbers in these comments** — the harness had omitted Tailwind preflight's `button { font: inherit }`. Numbers are re-measured and every citation now names the harness; the claims left standing are COMPARISONS (fork vs branch), which held across both harnesses, not absolutes, which did not. Counter: the pass decides. |

| 2026-09-04 | Chart readability — every chart states its window, and the velocity axis states its unit (#1088, build #1095; tracers T2–T4) | `VelocityChart.tsx` (`sr-only` caption + the exported `VelocityAxisUnit`) · `AggregateVelocityCard.tsx` (stacked caption + the same unit) · `RankingChart.tsx` (`rankingSpanLabel`, caption) · `ReputationTrendChart.tsx` (empty state) · `chart-readability.test.tsx` | **Presentation only — no data path, no bucketing, no migration, no flag.** `src/lib/kpi/fragments.ts` and the **Review-time anchor** are outside the edit surface on purpose (ADR 0019 / #688 / #757); a chart that buckets wrongly stays a separate `RISK=high` ticket. **The ticket's headline measurement was FALSE and a prior tracer killed it before any code was written:** it claimed four of five charts were "nothing at all" to a screen reader, on a grep for `aria-label` / `role="img"` / `<title>` that missed the `sr-only` `<table>` + `<caption>` pattern two charts use — a pattern that exposes the FULL data series and is therefore *stronger* than the summary the plan proposed to copy onto them. Every chart already spoke. What was missing was not the name but the **unit and the period inside it**, which is a different ticket wearing the same title, and T2 was re-cut against six verified gaps. **Two of those six shrank further on contact with the files, and both shrinks are the same lesson: a gap asserted from a grep is not a gap.** (a) G5 read "`RankingChart` states no window anywhere, on screen or in the caption" — but it already draws its dates on the x-axis (`RankingChart.tsx:133–137` at HEAD — the fork-point citation L108–L112 went stale the moment this branch inserted `rankingSpanLabel` above that block, which review F8 caught), so the fix is caption-only. **Review F6 then showed that premise is only half true, and the record is corrected here rather than left for a later session to trip over:** that axis draws one UNTHINNED label per date, unlike `weekShortLabel`, so at the real bi-weekly-over-12-months cadence it is ~26 labels in a ~190px band at 390px and they overlap into illegibility at BOTH intake widths. G5 is genuinely closed for the screen-reader path and only nominally for the sighted one; the honest fix is a span statement in `RankingCard`'s header, which is OUTSIDE this run's edit surface — recorded as follow-up, not passed off as done. (b) G4 demanded the two `Recent*` charts state the SAME window; they cannot, because one reads the windowed `RatingBreakdown` aggregate (#405) and the other the recent-review sample, so a shared phrase would mean changing what one COMPUTES — forbidden by invariant 1. It became a regression pin that passes today. **`RankingChart` gets a DATED span, not a week count**, and that distinction is the substantive design call here: ranking is *sampled*, not bucketed — `service.ts:292` / `aggregate-queries.ts:237` return every snapshot of the last 12 months — so "last N weeks" would have been a false label dressed as a fix. The two velocity charts share `trendSpanLabel` (UX audit D36's single owner, imported not copied) so a per-location chart and its aggregate peer cannot drift; the helper's home file is outside the surface, so its trend-specific NAME could not be corrected in this run and is recorded as follow-up. **The sharpest finding is a test-coverage one: the five promised titles cannot see the screen.** They read the accessible name and the `sr-only` table, and every plot is `aria-hidden` — so a chart could satisfy all five and still show bare unitless numbers to a sighted reader, which is precisely what G2 was. T3 therefore adds a sixth, non-promised title querying `svg text` directly. `VelocityAxisUnit` names the unit ONCE in the `PAD.t` headroom rather than suffixing three ticks or rotating an axis title, because the intake names 390px as the width where an axis label competes with the plot. Evidence is a real before/after render, not an assertion: a Vite harness with `@` aliased at two `src/` copies (the BEFORE tree is current `src/` with the four files restored from `2be0c323`), the repo's own Tailwind v4 pipeline and `console.css`, shot at 390px and 1440px under `reducedMotion`. **It exposed a PRE-EXISTING defect that is deliberately NOT fixed here:** at 390px the `avg 8.8` reference label overlaps the trend line and bars, in the BEFORE shot too. It is inside the edit surface and it is real, but it is not one of the six gaps, and inventing a seventh mid-run is the drift the tracer ordering exists to prevent — recorded for an owner ruling instead — **owner ruled it IN on 2026-09-04, so T5 carries it as an authorised addition rather than silent drift.** **T5 then closed the last two gaps and produced the run's sharpest lesson about `sr-only` copy.** G6: both `Recent*` charts labelled every row and named neither chart — a row label names a ROW, so a reader met five unattributed bars. The fix is the pattern already in the codebase, not a new one: both take the `sr-only` `<table>` + `<caption>` the plotted charts use, and their visual `<ul>` goes `aria-hidden` in the same move, which is what keeps "must not make the rows speak twice" true. Two announcement mechanisms became one across all four charts. **The first draft of those captions repeated the visible sub-label VERBATIM and broke two `AnalyticsContent` assertions by making `getByText` ambiguous — and the tests were right.** A duplicated `sr-only` sentence *is* a reader hearing the line twice: the same defect G6 warns about in different clothes. Both captions were reworded to state the same fact in different words, and the sentiment caption deliberately keeps "most recent" where the rating caption keeps "selected period", because G4's two windows are genuinely different and one shared phrase would misdescribe whichever chart it did not come from. `AnalyticsContent.test.tsx` sits outside the edit surface, so the component was the only place the fix could go — and the correct one. G3: the two velocity tables disagreed because **`weekShortLabel` THINS** — it returns `''` on odd indices so the axis does not crowd, which is right for an axis and wrong for a table where every row needs its own date; the per-location table papered over it with `Week 2` and the aggregate printed a raw ISO key. `weekRowLabel` calls the same formatter at index 0 of 1, taking it and its load-bearing UTC pin without the thinning; its natural home `velocity-math.ts` is outside the surface, and copying the formatter would have copied that pin and created a second owner for the very thing this ticket unifies — follow-up, not a duplicate. `VelocityDatum` now documents that `id` is the week-start ISO and `label` is the THINNED axis string; that contract was implicit, and implicit is how the two tables drifted. The stacked chart also drew **no x labels at all** and now draws them on its twin's rule and geometry. The `avg` label keeps its position on the average line — moving it would break what it points at — and gains a card-coloured stroke painted under the glyphs, verified in a fresh 390px render. One further pre-existing inconsistency is recorded and deliberately NOT touched: `RANKING_COLORS` hard-codes six hex literals while `tokens.css` declares `--rw-chart-1…6` and the two disagree past the first entry, which is a token-ownership question rather than a readability one. Counter: the pass decides. |

| 2026-09-04 | **Ledger attribution** moves onto the writer's side of the seam, so all seven **Refusal** lanes are covered by construction (#1083) | `src/lib/refusals/write.ts` (`captureLedgerWriteFault`, both write arms) · `worker/jobs/response-generation/persist.ts` (`withLedgerAttribution` narrowed, `held_draft` call deleted) · `CONTEXT.md` (**Ledger attribution**, LOCKED) · `docs/directory.md` (2 rows) · 2 test files (+16 cases) | **The architectural point is where the report lives, not that it exists.** Five of seven lanes surfaced a ledger fault as an ordinary job failure. #1079 had fixed two by wrapping their CALL SITES, and the obvious continuation was to wrap the other seven sites — which is what the ticket proposed. That is the wrong seam: a fault in `INSERT INTO refusal_events` is a property of THAT STATEMENT, and `write.ts` is its only owner (#1001), so call-site attribution puts the report on the far side of a seam that already existed. The deletion test settles it — delete `withLedgerAttribution` and the write-fault half reappears nowhere, while the resolve-fault half reappears at exactly ONE call site. So the writer takes the write fault (zero new interface members, zero new parameters, every lane covered, the omission unspellable, **five lane files not edited at all**), and the wrapper survives for one lane and one fault class: the `policy_gate_held` resolve, which throws before the writer is reached. The two can never both fire for one failure, which is what lets the `held_draft` wrapper be deleted outright rather than deduplicated. **THE PLAN GATE EARNED ITS COST HERE, AND THE SHARPEST FINDING IS THIS TICKET'S OWN DEFECT ONE LAYER IN.** Three adversarial rounds took the eval from 12 titles to 16. The set arm materializes its rows in a statement BEFORE the INSERT, and `input.occurredAt.toISOString()` raises `RangeError` on a `new Date(NaN)` — type-valid, so nothing upstream stops it. A guard around the SQL alone would have shipped a writer that cannot attribute its own row-construction failure. The guard now starts at the row map, its lane string is computed in the `catch` from `inputs` and never from `rows` (which may not exist when materialization is what failed), and `row_count` reads `inputs.length` for the same reason. The sibling trap is real and now test-protected: `uuidv7()` is interpolated INSIDE both tagged templates, so it already evaluates within the guard, and a tidy to `const id = uuidv7()` above the `try` moves it out silently — round 2's finding was that a prose rule against the hoist **is not a rule until a test enforces it**, which is the same defect class as the round-1 finding one level up. **Rounds 2 and 3 resumed round 1's Codex session: 120K tokens cold, then 16K and 19K — about 15% of a cold start each**, which is the measured argument for the workflow's ban on a cold verification round. **Two process lessons, both promoted to lab-notes.** (a) The subagent barrier is about the WORKING TREE, not only HEAD: a gate run against a clean `git status` while an agent was mid-flight read a file the agent wrote during the run, and the reclassify row reported `253 + 114 = 367` production lines for a change that had not been committed. A clean porcelain is a snapshot, not a lease. (b) This repo's docblocks cite each other ACROSS function boundaries, so narrowing a function stales prose in its callers — `recordPolicyGateRefusal`'s docblock cited an `await` the narrowed wrapper no longer has; grep the symbol, not just the function. Also recorded rather than argued away: the reclassify trigger fires at 495 raw changed lines against a 180 estimate, but `git diff -w` puts the substance at 179 — wrapping two long tagged SQL templates in `try` reindents every line of both, and the row cannot tell a reindent from new logic (same class as open #1114). Scope was attribution only; the ticket's second half — whether a ledger-attributed abort should short-circuit the retry ladder instead of re-spending the LLM draft and the moderation call — ships as its own ticket, which is the sequencing the ticket itself asks for. Counter: the pass decides. |

Fixed at the probe; neither consumer changed (#949) | `supabase/migrations/118_poller_heartbeats.sql` (new) · `src/lib/shared/poller-liveness.ts` (new) · `src/lib/responses/judge-health.ts` · `worker/background/pollers/JudgeSweepPoller.ts` · `worker/background/pollers/health-alert/checks.ts` · `scripts/ci/tenant-tables.ts` (`REVIEWED_NOT_TENANT_SCOPED`) · 5 test files (3 modified, 2 new) · `migrations-log.md` · 2 directory pages · spec `2026-07-13-ws6-judge-governor-design.md` §5 | **New table, no tenant column, no flag, no billing surface.** Five things a later pass should not re-derive. (a) **The docstring's "accepted blind spot (spec §5)" named covers that never covered it** — the kill-switch downgrade fires on the FLAG, not on the loop, and error density needs the very verdicts a dead sweep withholds; §5 is now marked CLOSED rather than left to mislead. (b) **The heartbeat's MEANING is decided by its position in the cycle, not by the statement**, so three unit cases pin the position: last statement (a throwing cycle records nothing), below the `judgeEnabled()` early return (a heartbeat means a real Judge cycle), and recorded on an IDLE cycle (the probe must never confuse "no Judge work due" with "no Judge"). The owner chose the strict reading over the cheaper one, and it costs a ≤60s window of `judge_unhealthy` downgrades when `JUDGE_ENABLED` flips off→on — accepted, and the reason `checkJudgeSweepLiveness` must gate on the same flag or page nonstop in prod. (c) **The age is computed on the DATABASE clock**: the worker and Postgres are different clocks and every other health signal thresholds on `NOW()`, and postgres.js truncates microseconds off a `timestamptz` — returning an integer age sidesteps that boundary rather than assuming it is harmless. (d) **A separate primary-key SELECT, deliberately NOT folded into the staleness query**: `tenant-scope-allowlist.ts` keys exemptions by file + table + `sqlSnippet`, so editing either existing statement breaks its match; one extra indexed lookup is cheaper than that churn (Codex round 2). (e) **`poller_heartbeats` is in `REVIEWED_NOT_TENANT_SCOPED` and deliberately ABSENT from `TABLE_DISPOSITIONS`** — `admin_audit_log` is classified only because `actor_user_id` is on the offboarding account-linked column scan, this table has no such column, and `table-disposition.test.ts` asserts EXACT equality with `TENANT_TABLES` + the explicit lists, so an entry would fail it (Codex round 1). **The change's real blast radius was the test surface, and it was found by the plan gate rather than by CI**: two live-DB suites run the REAL probe, and a fail-closed third arm silently flips their default answer — `judge-store.integration` would have kept its `toBe(false)` cases passing for the WRONG reason, and `release.integration` withholds every releasable case. Verified load-bearing rather than asserted: with the row cleared and the seed removed, that suite floods with `judge_unavailable`. The stale-heartbeat case runs inside a ROLLED-BACK transaction because the `judge_sweep` row is a process-wide singleton shared by every concurrent live-DB vitest process, and the suites upsert but never delete it for the same reason. Migration 118 was applied to **staging 2026-09-07T06:06Z and production 06:07Z**, byte-identical SQL, BEFORE this code merged — the probe reads the table unconditionally, so the reverse order throws `42P01` on every health call in both lanes. Verified on both by introspection (identical results: RLS true / FORCE off, zero policies, `has_table_privilege` false for `anon` and `authenticated`, sole index the pkey), never the ledger. Counter: the pass decides. |
| 2026-09-07 | docs/949-judge-flag-warmup — the **`JUDGE_ENABLED` off→on warm-up window** reaches the four places an operator actually reads before a production flip, and two stale "#949 is open" claims are corrected now that it is closed | `.claude/reference/env-vars.md` (the `JUDGE_ENABLED` entry — the OWNER of the fact) · `.claude/skills/flag-flip/SKILL.md` (coupling bullet, step 6 wait-and-verify, one Common mistake) · `docs/future-plans/2026-07-01-agentic-reputation-platform-design.md` (§8.4 ordered-prod-flip row; #949 dropped from the §9 surviving-debt tracker) · `docs/currents.md` | **Documentation only — no code, no schema, no flag was flipped.** The window is real behaviour created by #949: `isJudgeHealthy`'s liveness arm FAILS CLOSED on a missing heartbeat, and a flag-off `runJudgeSweepCycle` returns before writing one, so the flag's own OFF state is exactly the state the new arm reads as dead. For up to one **Poller cadence** (`JUDGE_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, 60s) after the flip the judge reads unavailable, auto-policy rows downgrade to owner review with `judge_unhealthy`, and the release phase withholds. **The failure mode being documented is not the window — it is the operator's reaction to it:** the symptom is indistinguishable from a broken flip, and reverting the flag re-arms it, so every location says "wait one cadence, do not flip back". **The downgrade is one-way** and that is stated wherever the wait is: a row already sent to owner review does not return to the auto lane when the heartbeat lands, which is the argument for flipping in a quiet window rather than merely waiting. **One owner, three pointers** (CLAUDE.md's one-authoritative-owner rule): `env-vars.md` carries the full semantics because the skill's own step 1 sends every flip there; the skill carries the PROCEDURE (wait, then run the verification query) and points back; §8.4 carries the step-specific trap for the one checklist that will actually execute this in prod. The verification query in the skill was **run against staging, not composed from memory** — it returned one `judge_sweep` row at `age_seconds` 33. Two corrections ride along because closing an issue without them leaves the docs asserting the opposite: `currents.md` said the blind spot "is filed as #949" and §9's tracker listed #949 as surviving debt "riding the §8.4 autonomy-flip checklist" — it now rides that checklist as a documented trap, not as an open gap. Historical records (the #631 review round, the handoffs, `progresstracking.md`) are left alone: they were true when written. Counter: the pass decides. |
| 2026-09-07 | feat/1183-admin-transport-seam — `requireAdminWithAal2` goes **dual-mode**: a **Verified admin** now resolves from EITHER **Admin transport**, through a new seam BELOW the gates that returns `{ authUserId, aal, transport }` and decides nothing. `requireAdminJson` and `resolveAdminActor` inherit it by composition; `requireRole` stays cookie-only, so the ~40 owner and mixed-role routes reaching it via `requireRoleOr401` do not widen. `ci:admin-aal2`'s allowlist and all three credited gate shapes are untouched (#1183, ADR 0025 D1–D4/D9/D10) | `src/lib/auth/admin-transport.ts` (new) · `src/lib/auth/verified-identity.ts` (new) · `src/lib/auth/admin-mfa-gate.ts` · `src/lib/db/auth.ts` (`loadAppUser` extracted from `getCurrentUser`) · 3 test files new/rewritten + **22 admin route suites** migrated (fixtures only) · `docs/directory/lib-platform.md` · `scripts/ci/tenant-scope-allowlist.ts` (one `sqlSnippet` re-spelled) | **No route file, no `src/proxy.ts`, no CSRF, no CORS, no migration, no flag, no dependency** — those are #1184/#1185/#1186. Five things a later pass should not re-derive. (a) **The transport branch is a seam below the gate, not a branch inside it**, and the predicate has ONE owner (`admin-transport.ts`, Edge-safe) because `src/proxy.ts` imports it next; two copies of "is this bearer mode" disagreeing is either a bypass or a dead route. (b) **`isBearerRequest` is header PRESENCE, not well-formedness.** The ticket's owed test binds it: a malformed `Authorization` beside a valid admin cookie must be refused, never silently authenticated by the cookie. `readBearerToken` answers the separate well-formedness question, so #1185 can pick either without the two ever diverging into an ACCEPTANCE. (c) **`getUser(jwt)` is not an optimization to remove** — `getClaims` verifies a signature locally and is blind to revocation, so a claims-only bearer would keep a signed-out admin working for up to `JWT_EXP` (3600s) on the control plane. Two tests exist solely to fail if it is dropped, one of them with a valid cookie present so a fallback fails too. (d) **The error classification was wrong THREE times before it was right, and the SDK is why.** `getClaims` returns `AuthError`s and RETHROWS everything else, so "thrown ⇒ 500" swallows nothing useful and mislabels plenty: a GoTrue HTTP 500 arrives as a RETURNED `AuthApiError(status 500)` (only 502/503/504/520-524/530 become `AuthRetryableFetchError`), while `decodeJWT`'s `JSON.parse` lets an attacker-supplied `ew.e30.AA` escape as a raw `SyntaxError`. A live probe against the installed SDK — not a reading — settled those two, and Codex round 3 found the same shapes independently. **The third was found by the REVIEW WAVE, by two axes at once, and it is the one that mattered most: `handleError` raises a THIRD shape, `AuthUnknownError`, when `await error.json()` throws** — an HTML 502 from a CDN, a WAF block page, a truncated body — and it is neither of the other two names, so the shipped-to-review classifier turned exactly that outage into 401s: the "wall of bad credentials" the classifier exists to prevent. The rule is now scope plus a complete taxonomy: the seam's `try` wraps each SDK await and NOTHING else, a throw from either is a 401 (the malformed-token boundary, or an unauthenticated caller forces 500s on the admin plane with a two-character token), and the 500 arm covers all three returned shapes. Expiry moved to a data channel (`allowExpired: true` + an explicit `exp` test) rather than being matched on the message `JWT has expired`, per this repo's match-on-code rule. **The classification applies to the BEARER arm ONLY** — review also caught it silently moving two cookie answers (Supabase fault 401→500, AAL fault →500) on the path ADR 0025 D4 forbids touching; reverted and pinned. **And the "500 someone is paged for" justification was itself unproven**: `internalError` only `console.error`s, nothing captures it, and no runbook row watches admin 5xx. The honest claim, now written everywhere it appeared, is that the SIGNAL IS CORRECT and greppable; routing it to an alert sink is its own ticket. (e) **The refusal table moved two cookie answers** — a verified session with no `users` row is now 403 `Forbidden` (was 401; the old path could not tell "no session" from "no row") and an unreadable AAL is now `mfa_check_failed` (was `mfa_required`) — because the ticket declares that table for BOTH transports. No existing test pinned either old answer. **The migration produced the run's most transferable lesson:** the 22 suites mock the gate's dependencies BY NAME, and the first shim made `mockRequireRole` a double consumer, so a suite arming its 403 with `mockRejectedValueOnce` had the rejection eaten by the seam shim and its authorization test silently asserted a SUCCESS status instead. Three of four migration agents hit it independently; the unified shim reads `mock.results.at(-1)` so the mock is invoked exactly once per gate pass and a `...Once` control keeps its meaning. Also recorded: `docs/directory/lib-platform.md` sat at exactly 2,998/3,000 words, so the two new files forced the `auth/` section onto the row shape CLAUDE.md prescribes (WHERE + traps, WHY delegated); the page is at its cap again, and the next `src/lib` file under it needs a page split, which is a `docs/directory.md` change. Counter: the pass decides. |
| 2026-09-08 | feat/1182-admin-reset — `scripts/revoke-admin.ts`, the **admin revoke**: the #1182 reset removes every admin's access as a BAN on the Supabase Auth identity, never a row deletion, and `--restore` is the exact reversal | `scripts/revoke-admin.ts` (new) · `scripts/mint-admin.ts` (two helpers exported, no behaviour change) · `scripts/ci/__tests__/revoke-admin.test.ts` + `…integration.test.ts` (new) · `docs/runbooks/mint-admin.md` § Revoking and restoring an admin · `.claude/reference/env-vars.md` (`MFA_ENFORCEMENT_ENABLED` staging flip) | **No migration, no route, no `src/proxy.ts`, no new env var, no new dependency.** Four things a later pass should not re-derive. (a) **A revoke can never be a delete**: `users` rows are FK targets (`businesses.onboarded_by`, `impersonation_sessions.actor_user_id` NOT NULL, `admin_audit_log.actor_user_id` NOT NULL, the `014`/`034` actor columns), so the only Postgres write in the whole script is the audit INSERT — the live-DB suite asserts `to_jsonb(users)` is identical before and after a revoke+restore, role included. (b) **The ticket asked for `account_audit_log` via `writeAccountAuditLog` and the schema forbids it**: that table's `account_id` is NOT NULL and an admin's is NULL, which is verbatim why migration 117 exists. `admin_revoked`/`admin_restored` go to `admin_audit_log` with the same `bootstrap_self_actor` shape `admin_minted` uses, because a full reset may leave no other admin to name as actor. (c) **Idempotency reads `auth.users.banned_until`, not the Auth admin API's user payload** — GoTrue OMITS `banned_until` from that JSON when it is null (probed against staging 2026-09-08: the key is simply absent), so absence there cannot be distinguished from a version that does not serialize it. The column read carries the `to_regclass` guard `probeAuthIdentity` established, because the test databases provision schema `auth` with no `auth.users`. (d) **Ordering is Auth-then-audit and deliberately NOT wrapped in `sql.begin`**: one Postgres write not paired with a Postgres state change gains nothing from a transaction but the appearance of atomicity across a boundary Postgres does not span. An audit row written first would claim a revocation that may never happen; a ban whose audit row failed is real, reported, and repairable — and is NOT rolled back, because restoring a login to tidy a ledger is the worse outcome. The residual is stated rather than hidden in both the script and the runbook: an access token minted before the ban stays valid until it expires (JWT expiry, 1h default), because a ban blocks sign-in and refresh, not an issued JWT. Counter: the pass decides. |

Plan rounds keep the hash, but intake `risk=high` always owes the round — a prose-only fix can move an invariant under an unchanged heading. Both light lanes now RUN the hook (`lefthook run pre-push --force`), so the list has one owner. `--force` is load-bearing and was found the hard way: without it lefthook skips every check when no commit is waiting to be pushed and exits 0 in 0.3 s, which would have replaced one false-green gate with another; a parity test pins the flag. (3) **The gate built only for ui/mixed while AGENTS.md requires a build for any behavior change**; it now builds for every non-docs-only change, and fast mode keeps its "nothing the build compiles moved" skip. Owner kept the AGENTS.md rule rather than relaxing it: `src/lib` is shared with the app, so "backend" is not a build boundary. One owner now (execution-lanes § Tracer bullets and TDD); the parity test pins that neither router restates it or asks for complete code. (2) **`ci:weakened-checks` reads the diff**, because a skipped test or a stripped assertion is invisible in the final tree. FAIL is limited to relaxations the repo carries zero of today (focused test, disabled test, lowered `DIFF_COVERAGE_MIN`); everything contextual is a REVIEW row that never fails and that the reviewer must answer — a gate on "assertions decreased" would be answered with meaningless assertions. (3) **Red-team SKILL.md 4,192 → 2,923 words**: five closed branch-pinned budget grants moved verbatim to a reference; the runner still enforces each in code. (4) **Three skills adapted from addyosmani/agent-skills @ `c004a74` (MIT)**, read in full first. Deliberately NOT adopted: the pack's router, its `/spec`…`/ship` commands, and `CONSTRAINTS.md` — a second owner for the quality bar that `package.json` `ci:*` and the wiring test already own. Rejected by the same research: replacing `/feature` with a generic pack, a paid third-party harness, cross-harness cost accounting, and a 12–20 task bake-off; `docs/agents/change-ledger.md` (one row per shipped change, reviewed after 10) replaces the last. Next pass: whether the Claude lane's "never fix the task by hand" still disagrees with the shared "root implements the first tracer" (research finding 1, not addressed here). Counter unchanged — tooling only. |
| 2026-09-18 | /feature — explicit model routing (owner-directed; same branch as the row above) | `references/execution-lanes.md` § Model routing (new, one owner for both harnesses) · both routers · `feature-small.md` · `feature-swarm.md` · `.claude/settings.json` (`env.CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=sonnet`) · `feature-lane-parity.test.ts` (+5) · `docs/agents/change-ledger.md` | Workflow tooling only. **An unnamed Claude subagent inherits the lead's model** (resolution order: per-invocation `model` → agent definition → `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` → main conversation), so a frontier lead session made every grep helper a frontier call, and the router's old "fast / standard / most capable" sentence named no model and bound nothing. Four tiers now map to exact IDs on both sides (`haiku`/`gpt-5.6-luna` → `sonnet`/`gpt-5.6-terra` → `opus`/`gpt-5.6-sol` → `fable`/`gpt-6-astra`; Codex IDs verified against `~/.codex/sessions`, not recalled). The project default is `sonnet` and deliberately NOT forced, so the lead can still raise a review axis. Guards against false economy: a reviewer is never weaker than the author and never below tier 3; authorization, tenancy, transaction, migration, idempotency and concurrency are never DECIDED below tier 3; cost never changes the gate. A command's `model:` frontmatter lasts one turn, so it is not used — the session default is the real lever, and the ledger's `Lead` column records the tier so the 10-change review can see which routes held. Counter unchanged — tooling only. |
| 2026-09-18 | /feature — always-visible progress (owner-directed; same branch as the two rows above) | `.agents/skills/feature/scripts/progress.mjs` (`statusline` subcommand) · `lib/feature-snapshot.mjs` (`renderStatusLine`) · `references/progress-and-live-view.md` · `feature-workflow-runtime.test.ts` (+6) | Workflow tooling only. The step ladder printed only at phase boundaries, so between boundaries the owner saw nothing without asking. A third READER of the one ledger (`.feature/progress.json`), not a second ledger: `renderStatusLine` takes the same snapshot as the block, so the two cannot disagree. It must never break the bar — no run, no repository and a corrupt file all print nothing and exit 0 — and it skips the fingerprint check because that spawns a process on every refresh. Wiring lives in the owner's personal `~/.claude/statusline.sh`, outside the repository, and degrades to an absent segment against a checkout whose `progress.mjs` predates the subcommand. | 2026-09-18 | red-team — the ordinary mutation budget is a clock, not a count (owner-directed after a measured analysis of the feat/safety-review-auto-approval run; no /feature run for the workflow tooling itself) | `.agents/skills/red-team/scripts/mutation-runner.mjs` (`ROUND_TIME_BUDGET_MS`, `ordinaryTimeSpentMs`, `MAX_RUNS_PER_ROUND` 30 → 400 backstop, `--timeout` bound enforced) · both `red-team/SKILL.md` copies · `red-team-mutation-runner.test.ts` | Workflow tooling only. **Measured, not estimated:** on #1311 a mutation launch took 2.8 s (mean and max over 60 launches), so the 30-launch cap — sized for the 120 s worst case, an hour — stopped each round after 84 s of testing. An 85-row inventory took three rounds (18 + 25 + 24 min of agent time), a 73-minute wait for the owner's third-round grant, and still ended `INCOMPLETE_BUDGET` with 28 rows unmeasured. #1185, #1189, #1190 and Runs C, D and E each needed a one-off extension for the same cause; this removes the cause instead of granting a seventh. The ordinary budget is now 20 minutes of MEASURED test time per round, checked before each launch, so a round is bounded at budget + one timeout; a launch that never recorded its end is charged its whole timeout, because an unknown cost is the worst one. 400 launches stays as a backstop against a loop of instant commands. "≤120 s per launch" was prose on the ordinary path and is now enforced, since the round bound depends on it. **Deliberately untouched:** every owner-granted budget stays a launch count with its own literal-30 checkpoints, and granted launches write the journal byte-for-byte as before (no `elapsedMs`/`timeoutMs`), because their admission proofs compare it; `red-team-status.mjs` never enforced the ordinary count and is unchanged; the round caps (two, a third on a recorded grant) are unchanged. The skill now orders the inventory by risk, so whatever the clock leaves unmeasured is the least important part. **Found while testing this change:** a budget fixture with `pgid: 1` made the test file's shared teardown run `process.kill(-1, 'SIGKILL')` and logged the owner out three times. Fixed at both ends — the teardown and the runner's `reapGroup` now refuse any pgid that is not an integer above 1 (the journal is a file, so its pgid is input) — with a stubbed-`kill` test and a lab-notes entry. Not done: a batch mode for the runner (one model turn per mutation step is the remaining ~20 min per round) — a larger change to a safety tool, deferred until the ledger shows it matters. Counter unchanged — tooling only. |
| 2026-09-19 | /feature — model handoff at plan approval (owner-directed; no /feature run for the workflow tooling itself) | `progress.mjs` (`handoff` subcommand) · `lib/feature-snapshot.mjs` (`renderModelHandoff`, `EXECUTE_LEAD`, `handoffDocPath`) · `references/execution-lanes.md` (§ Model handoff at plan approval, the owner) · both routers · both `feature-resume` commands · `progress-and-live-view.md` · `feature-workflow-runtime.test.ts` (+5) · `feature-lane-parity.test.ts` (+3) | Workflow tooling only. The owner starts sessions on a frontier model (`fable`, `gpt-6-astra`) for the intake and the plan, and wants implementation on tier 3. A session cannot change its own model and `/clear` keeps it, so the switch has to be a NEW session; the existing step 3→4 exit was optional ("when the session is heavy") and named no model. Now a tier-4 planning session records the phase, writes the handoff doc, runs `progress.mjs handoff`, prints its output verbatim and STOPS. The block is printed, never composed: the launch line on the tier-3 model plus `/feature-resume <branch>` (or `$feature-resume`), and it refuses unless the phase is `plan_approved` and the handoff doc exists, because a fresh session has only the disk. **The owner pasting the prompt IS the sign-off to implement**, so the resume commands go straight to step 4 with no second approval pause. The script's two model IDs are a copy of the tier-3 row that execution-lanes.md owns; a test fails if they drift. Skipped for a session already on tier 3 or below, for `/feature-swarm` subagents, and for the light lanes (no plan; tier 2 from the start). Counter unchanged — tooling only. |
| 2026-09-19 | P3 batch #1103 / #1113 / #1114 (owner-directed sweep of nine P3 issues; #494 verified already fixed and closed, #122 and #235 closed won't-do) | `scripts/verify-gate.sh` (new `reclassify_bucket`, two added exclusions, two comment blocks) · `scripts/ci/__tests__/gate-rows.test.ts` (+2 cases) · `docs/directory/lib-solicitation-c.md` (one stale sentence) · the two narrow title guards under `src/app/onboarding/_shared/__tests__/` and `src/app/mfa/__tests__/` | Three unrelated P3s, disjoint surfaces, one PR. **#1114 is the one with a rule in it:** the `reclassify` WARN now excludes `docs/runbooks/evidence/**` and exactly two `.claude/reference` paths — `arch-review-log.md` and `lab-notes/**` — alongside the plans and reviews it already skipped, on the argument the exclusion already stated: a run cannot commit scope creep against paperwork the lane REQUIRES it to produce. **Round-1 review narrowed this**: the first cut used `.claude/reference/*`, which also exempted `env-vars.md` and `migrations-log.md` — the registry half of a new flag or migration, i.e. real in-scope work — so the glob was replaced by the two mandated paths and two test rows now fence it. On #1082 that paperwork was 108 lines against a 27-line executable diff and the estimate was inflated 35 → 90 → 250 to keep the row green, which is the measurement ceasing to measure. Kept narrow: only the `evidence/` subtree of `docs/runbooks` is paperwork, and a test pins that a runbook itself still counts as production. The classifier became a FUNCTION rather than an inline `case` so the test runs it — this file's own row-wiring suite records a source-text assertion passing against a disarmed mutant. Review found the first cut tested only the CLASSIFIER, so swapping the caller's `test)` and `*)` arms would still pass; the accumulation loop is now `reclassify_totals`, fed a real numstat transcript, and writing that test surfaced a genuine defect the inline version had hidden — a plain `while read` drops the final line of a transcript with no trailing newline, which `git diff` happens to never produce. **#1103 was partly wrong as filed:** it claimed two narrow guards duplicated the app-wide scan's suffix check; only the onboarding one did. The mfa guard asserts an exact title value and a no-second-owner claim, neither derivable from "does not end in the brand", so one assertion was removed and both guards now state in a header comment what they alone own — including the honest residual: the old regex was FILE-wide and the app-wide scan is TITLE-scoped, so a stray brand suffix in body copy or an `openGraph` title is now caught by nothing, accepted because the bug being guarded is a doubled TITLE. **On #1113's open question** — whether `ci:directory-map`'s presence-only check should also police prose — the answer taken here is NO: prose accuracy is not mechanically checkable, and a row's sentence is exactly the kind of claim a review reads. The audit stays a presence check. Counter unchanged — no non-test file under `src/`, `worker/` or `supabase/`. |
| 2026-09-19 | refactor/616-fallow-complexity — the two pre-existing fallow complexity findings #616 tracks are cleared by DECOMPOSITION, not suppression. `logger.ts` `emit` (cognitive 24, CRAP 106.4, severity **critical**) becomes assembly over six module-private helpers with its external interface untouched; `handleOnboardingDemo` (cognitive 25, cyclomatic 19, 144 lines) becomes orchestration only at cognitive 7 / cyclomatic 8, with the subscription guard and the two best-effort cost steps moved into the existing `onboarding-demo/` DI package | `src/lib/shared/logger.ts` · `worker/jobs/OnboardingDemoWorker.ts` · `worker/jobs/onboarding-demo/{subscription-guard,cost-reconcile}.ts` (new, shipping their `docs/directory` rows) · three test files (+28 tests, no pre-existing test BODY changed) · `scripts/ci/tenant-scope-allowlist.ts` (three entries follow the query to its new file — same SQL, same `od.id` scoping) · the `logger.ts` directory row, whose stated signature `createWorkerLogger(correlationId)` was wrong | **Behavior-preserving. No schema, migration, flag, env var or dependency.** Characterization-first: T1/T3 pinned the contract against the UNREFACTORED code before either extraction, so a red test means the refactor moved behavior, never that the test is stale. **Two mutation probes recorded**, each failing exactly one new test and passing the pre-#616 suite: `await completeDemo` → `void completeDemo`, and `outboxAttempt: undefined` in the rewire. The first is why T3 test 1 holds a hand-resolved promise instead of asserting `mock.invocationCallOrder`, which passes that mutation; the second is why a root-level test runs the REAL guard, since the helper's own test starts after the boundary the extraction creates. Three Codex plan rounds on ONE resumed session (REVISE → REVISE → APPROVED; 13 → 3 → 0 shell commands, which is the argument for resuming a verification round rather than starting it cold). **Round 1 exposed a FALSE invariant in the signed-off intake**: it claimed every non-completing path releases the 20¢ reserve, but the subscription SELECT, `claimDemo` and `markOutboxCompleted` sit outside the outer try and release nothing. Narrowed to the as-built contract and pinned by a test, rather than "fixed" — changing it would be a behavior change on a behavior-preserving branch. **A CRAP trap worth knowing:** fallow assumes 40% coverage when no coverage file is present, so an intermediate state at cognitive 9 / cyclomatic 10 still failed on CRAP 31.6 (10² × 0.6³ + 10). Real coverage cleared it, but that makes the proof depend on a coverage file being current inside a parallel gate; a `leavesNoWorkToDo` predicate took cyclomatic to 8 so the check passes with no coverage data at all. Counter unchanged until merged. |
